Guidance traceability

Every product rule is listed here with the automated checks that enforce it. tests/test_guidance.py fails if a referenced test or CI step disappears, so a rule cannot lose its check without this table changing in the same review.

Reference formats: tests/<file>.py::<test> for a pytest test, and ci:<step or job name> for a GitHub Actions step or job.

ID Rule Enforced by
G01 Offline by default: no web, no cluster, no telemetry unless enabled tests/test_config_net.py::test_defaults_are_offline, tests/test_debug.py::test_cluster_disabled_by_default, tests/test_server_agent_cli.py::test_toolkit_availability_gates
G02 Admin offline lock overrides webEnabled, blocks corpus downloads, allows only local models, and only tightens tests/test_offline_lock.py::test_lock_beats_web_enabled_everywhere, tests/test_offline_lock.py::test_lock_allows_only_local_models, tests/test_offline_lock.py::test_lock_only_tightens, ci:Chart guards (fail-closed NetworkPolicy, fsGroup only off-OpenShift, web flag wired to env), tests/test_review_regressions.py::test_any_true_spelling_locks, tests/test_review_regressions.py::test_unreadable_system_config_fails_closed, tests/test_review_regressions.py::test_locked_model_client_never_leaves_loopback, tests/test_review_regressions.py::test_lock_locality_is_strict, tests/test_review_regressions.py::test_corpus_download_is_refused_by_the_lock_itself
G03 Web, when enabled: HTTPS GET only, allowlisted domains and redirects, text content only, size-capped, never written to disk or executed tests/test_offline_lock.py::test_web_reads_text_only_and_only_with_get, tests/test_config_net.py::test_domain_allowlist_applies_to_redirects, tests/test_config_net.py::test_web_blocked_when_disabled, tests/test_security_hardening.py::test_web_fetch_stops_at_max_bytes, tests/test_offline_lock.py::test_web_fetch_bounds_compressed_bodies_and_odd_charsets
G04 Posture is provable: doctor reports reach and fails on contradictions tests/test_offline_lock.py::test_doctor_reports_posture_and_flags_conflicts
G05 Cluster access is read-only, Secrets are never read, values are redacted tests/test_debug.py::test_secrets_never_allowed, tests/test_debug.py::test_redaction, tests/test_security_hardening.py::test_cluster_redaction_covers_values_args_and_list_wrappers, ci:Helm test (in-cluster readiness through the Service)
G06 Diagnose before change: read-only steps (get, describe, logs, bundle) first; exec and changes need approval tests/test_diagnose_first.py::test_classify_step, tests/test_diagnose_first.py::test_playbook_checks_never_mutate, tests/test_diagnose_first.py::test_diagnosis_orders_read_only_steps_first_and_gates_the_rest, tests/test_diagnose_first.py::test_bundle_writes_redacted_read_only_evidence, tests/test_review_regressions.py::test_classify_segments, tests/test_diagnose_first.py::test_diagnose_cli_takes_pasted_text_a_file_or_stdin
G07 Commands meant for copy-paste never carry shell comments (zsh would pass them as arguments) tests/test_diagnose_first.py::test_no_copyable_command_carries_a_shell_comment, tests/test_diagnose_first.py::test_split_comment, tests/test_review_regressions.py::test_every_playbook_command_is_paste_safe
G08 Specific root causes outrank generic symptoms tests/test_diagnose_first.py::test_specific_cause_outranks_generic_crashloop, tests/test_debug.py::test_diagnose_top_match, tests/test_diagnose_first.py::test_x509_rbac_and_platform_specific_steps
G09 Learning file: confirmed notes only, no secrets/logs/manifests, upgrade-safe, never exported tests/test_learning_file.py::test_confirmed_note_persists_and_is_searchable, tests/test_learning_file.py::test_rejects_credentials_manifests_and_raw_logs, tests/test_learning_file.py::test_older_file_loads_after_upgrade_and_keeps_unknown_keys, tests/test_learning_file.py::test_newer_or_corrupt_files_are_never_clobbered, tests/test_learning_file.py::test_mcp_note_requires_user_confirmation_and_never_exports, tests/test_learning_file.py::test_tools_count_patterns_not_content, tests/test_review_regressions.py::test_note_filter_rejects_secrets_manifests_and_logs, tests/test_review_regressions.py::test_concurrent_processes_never_lose_writes, tests/test_review_regressions.py::test_malformed_learning_file_is_contained, tests/test_review_regressions.py::test_two_corrupt_loads_keep_both_backups, tests/test_review_regressions.py::test_notes_reject_even_a_single_log_or_event_line
G10 Every design request runs the 7-step protocol tests/test_demos.py::test_protocol_runs_all_seven_steps_in_order, tests/test_generate.py::test_clarification_gate, tests/test_generate.py::test_protocol_takes_version_from_blueprint, tests/test_generate.py::test_protocol_takes_version_from_the_request_text
G11 Generated and fixed YAML is audited clean and accepted by a real API server tests/test_generate.py::test_generated_bundles_are_clean, tests/test_audit.py::test_fixer_resolves_blockers, ci:Server-side dry-run of generated manifests
G12 Answers cite credited sources; the reward is deterministic, resists gaming and credits a citation only when the cited chunk supports the sentence tests/test_reward_hardening.py::test_citation_to_the_right_doc_but_wrong_chunk_earns_no_credit, tests/test_knowledge_learn.py::test_citations_inside_knowledge_docs_resolve, tests/test_reward_hardening.py::test_qa_gaming_attempts_score_low, tests/test_reward_hardening.py::test_irrelevant_valid_citation_earns_no_credit, tests/test_evals_jira.py::test_uncited_answer_gets_no_credit_when_required, tests/test_knowledge_learn.py::test_citation_extraction

G14 Local or frontier models plug in; failures are clean tests/test_coverage_paths.py::test_openai_compatible_provider, tests/test_security_hardening.py::test_unreachable_local_model_fails_cleanly, tests/test_server_agent_cli.py::test_agent_loop_with_tools
G15 Learners are graded offline by production rules tests/test_knowledge_learn.py::test_reference_solutions_pass, tests/test_knowledge_learn.py::test_starters_fail, tests/test_knowledge_learn.py::test_tutor_flow_and_credits
G16 At least 5 offline demos, each under 10 seconds, isolated from user data tests/test_demos.py::test_demo_list, tests/test_demos.py::test_each_demo_runs_offline_fast_and_isolated, tests/test_demos.py::test_examples_ship_in_the_wheel, tests/test_demos.py::test_every_scenario_has_a_recorded_tape, ci:Render demo GIFs, ci:Install wheel in a clean venv and smoke-test
G17 MCP surface is strict: unknown arguments rejected, HTTP needs a token, host checks, body limit tests/test_server_agent_cli.py::test_mcp_rejects_unknown_arguments, tests/test_server_agent_cli.py::test_http_app_auth_and_health, tests/test_server_agent_cli.py::test_http_host_checks, tests/test_server_agent_cli.py::test_http_body_limit_and_audit_log, ci:MCP end-to-end over HTTP
G18 Releases are consistent and publishable: one version everywhere, dists pass the publish action's twine tests/test_distribution.py::test_plugin_pins_the_release, tests/test_distribution.py::test_mcp_registry_metadata, tests/test_bump_version.py::test_bump_updates_every_reference, tests/test_distribution.py::test_image_build_context_has_every_wheel_input, ci:publishable

G20 Docs stay focused: README under 600 words, use cases, learning and guidance linked tests/test_guidance.py::test_docs_are_concise_and_linked

G23 Answers stay right across Kubernetes, Helm, OpenShift 4.22, ROSA HCP, RHACS and everyday errors: a 61-question battery must pass in full tests/test_selftest.py::test_selftest_battery_passes_in_full, tests/test_selftest.py::test_selftest_cases_are_well_formed
G24 One product, four surfaces: MCP server, Claude plugin, CLI, OpenShift service/sidecar tests/test_distribution.py::test_ships_as_mcp_plugin_cli_and_openshift_service, tests/test_server_agent_cli.py::test_mcp_in_process, ci:Helm test (in-cluster readiness through the Service), ci:Audit the sidecar example
G25 Bring your own runbooks: org docs are indexed offline, credentials masked, cited, never exported by default tests/test_org_docs.py::test_runbooks_are_indexed_masked_and_cited, tests/test_org_docs.py::test_add_docs_cli_works_under_the_offline_lock
G26 Cadences: weekly risk review; the Red Hat Jira main copy every other day by default (daily with JIRA_EVERY_DAYS=1), continuing the previous encrypted copy and failing instead of replacing it when that copy does not decrypt; the docs corpus weekly; ROX Jira corpus is 1000+ issues from 4.10 onward, credited tests/test_distribution.py::test_scheduled_cadences_and_rox_corpus_scope, tests/test_evals_jira.py::test_classify_versions
G27 The Claude plugin runs the bundled, readable stdio server with every dependency pinned by hash, installs no LLM SDK, sets no package-manager variable, reads no credential, keeps state out of the plugin root and fits the directory's limits (files under 256 KiB, readable text, under 512 files) tests/test_plugin.py::test_bundled_server_matches_the_source, tests/test_plugin.py::test_mcp_config_runs_the_bundled_source_from_the_lock, tests/test_plugin.py::test_lock_pins_every_dependency_with_registry_and_hashes, tests/test_plugin.py::test_plugin_installs_no_llm_sdk, tests/test_plugin.py::test_plugin_fits_the_directory_limits, tests/test_plugin.py::test_icon_is_small_and_carries_no_shell_like_text, tests/test_plugin.py::test_manifest_uses_only_fields_the_plugin_reference_defines, tests/test_plugin.py::test_bundle_reads_no_credentials_from_the_machine, tests/test_plugin.py::test_bundle_has_no_download_and_execute_pattern, tests/test_plugin.py::test_bundle_names_no_credential_variables, ci:Plugin server copy matches src, ci:Plugin lock matches its pyproject, ci:Plugin dependency vulnerability audit, ci:Plugin MCP server end to end (as .mcp.json runs it)

G29 Runs on OpenShift 4.20 and later under restricted-v2 and Pod Security restricted; egress is DNS only by default with an optional EgressFirewall; the sidecar is loopback-only; requests are sized from measurements and nothing from a request is retained tests/test_distribution.py::test_chart_egress_firewall_mirrors_the_web_allowlist, tests/test_distribution.py::test_sidecar_is_loopback_only_with_dns_only_egress, ci:Sidecar under Pod Security restricted (loopback only, transcript), ci:Audit our own chart (OpenShift 4.20, 4.21, 4.22, Route, live cluster tools, EgressFirewall), ci:Chart guards (fail-closed NetworkPolicy, fsGroup only off-OpenShift, web flag wired to env), ci:Audit the sidecar example

G31 Second red-team review: no caller content in logs, opaque crashes, auth before any body is read, no caller regex compiled, no shared note writes over HTTP, no download-and-run notes, isolated plugin interpreter, least-privilege RBAC and web egress tests/test_review_regressions.py::test_tool_failures_never_log_caller_content, tests/test_review_regressions.py::test_unexpected_errors_are_opaque_to_client_and_log, tests/test_review_regressions.py::test_requests_without_the_token_are_rejected_before_the_body_is_read, tests/test_review_regressions.py::test_inline_grade_patterns_are_literal_text, tests/test_review_regressions.py::test_shared_http_servers_offer_no_learning_writes, tests/test_review_regressions.py::test_notes_never_carry_download_and_run_commands, tests/test_plugin.py::test_mcp_config_runs_the_bundled_source_from_the_lock, tests/test_review_regressions.py::test_global_options_before_the_subcommand_are_honoured

G33 Explains why a request was denied, policy by policy (Kyverno, Gatekeeper, ValidatingAdmissionPolicy, Pod Security, SCC, RHACS, quota, LimitRange, RBAC), with read-only checks, the fix and the matching audit rules; never treats an everyday error as a denial; says whether a NetworkPolicy allows a connection, which rule decides it, and proposes an allow policy that admits exactly that traffic; text from logs and manifests never puts shell syntax or terminal escapes into a command or the terminal, and hostile input cannot make it slow tests/test_policy.py::test_kyverno_block_with_folded_lines_names_policy_rule_path_and_rule_ids, tests/test_policy.py::test_pod_security_patch_clears_the_same_audit_findings, tests/test_policy.py::test_scc_names_the_fields_restricted_v2_rejected_and_the_uid_range, tests/test_policy.py::test_rbac_grant_is_exactly_the_missing_permission_and_audits_clean, tests/test_policy.py::test_every_policy_step_is_read_only_paste_safe_and_redacted, tests/test_policy.py::test_everyday_errors_are_not_policy_denials, tests/test_policy.py::test_reachability_on_the_shop_example, tests/test_policy.py::test_the_proposed_policy_allows_exactly_that_traffic, tests/test_policy.py::test_live_reachability_reads_only_what_it_needs, tests/test_policy.py::test_hostile_names_never_reach_a_command_line, tests/test_policy.py::test_cli_drops_terminal_escapes, tests/test_policy.py::test_explain_stays_fast_on_hostile_input
G34 Audit findings name the NIST SP 800-53 Rev. 5 controls they are evidence about (every rule mapped, every control titled, the compliance guide in step with the code); crypto is SHA-256, PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256, an HMAC compare, TLS through ssl and AES-256-GCM through cryptography (see G292), and doctor reports FIPS mode tests/test_controls.py::test_every_rule_maps_to_titled_800_53_controls, tests/test_controls.py::test_findings_and_reports_carry_controls, tests/test_compliance_doc.py::test_every_rule_has_exactly_one_row_and_every_row_has_a_rule, tests/test_controls.py::test_crypto_is_fips_friendly_and_reported
G35 The incident watch reads only pods, events, deployments and replicasets, attributes each problem to its workload, explains a new or recurring incident once, names what changed in the last rollout, resolves only after successful reads, brings back confirmed fixes on recurrence, and never stores log lines or Secret values tests/test_incidents.py::test_observe_attributes_every_signal_to_its_workload, tests/test_incidents.py::test_what_changed_names_the_rollout_and_the_diff, tests/test_incidents.py::test_poll_opens_explains_and_stores_nothing_sensitive, tests/test_incidents.py::test_recurrence_brings_back_the_confirmed_fix, tests/test_incidents.py::test_failed_reads_never_resolve_and_old_incidents_age_out, tests/test_incidents.py::test_tools_cli_and_service_wiring, ci:Incident watch finds and explains the crash loop and a Pod Security denial (read-only)
G36 Keeps a local copy of Red Hat's public Jira through its REST API only, one request per second or slower within a request budget, resuming without losing updates; says what became of a matching ticket (fixed and whether the cluster's version has it, open, not a bug, won't fix, duplicate) with the comment that explains it; never matches an everyday error; never stores reporter, assignee or author names and never ships the copy tests/test_tickets.py::test_a_fixed_bug_with_the_fix_and_workaround, tests/test_tickets.py::test_not_a_bug_says_so_with_the_reason, tests/test_tickets.py::test_generic_errors_stay_unmatched_even_when_some_tickets_paste_describe_output, tests/test_tickets.py::test_operator_versions_are_not_compared_with_openshift_versions, tests/test_tickets.py::test_sync_copies_every_status_then_only_changes, tests/test_tickets.py::test_each_project_measures_its_window_from_its_own_start, tests/test_tickets.py::test_a_budget_stops_cleanly_and_the_next_run_resumes, tests/test_tickets.py::test_retries_wait_at_least_the_crawl_delay, tests/test_tickets.py::test_the_sync_client_only_reads_from_the_jira_host, tests/test_tickets.py::test_hostile_records_and_odd_paths_never_break_answers, ci:The image answers from a local Red Hat Jira copy (SQLite FTS5 present)
G37 Capability tiers: everything beyond reading (probe, dry-run, external writes, gate forwarding) is off by default, shown by doctor, refused under the offline lock when it leaves the cluster, and each action needs a person's approval for that action, unexpired and used once; the read-only backends cannot write; tier Roles render only when enabled and kube-noob's own audit flags what they allow tests/test_tiers.py::test_every_tier_is_off_by_default, tests/test_tiers.py::test_enabled_tiers_and_their_contradictions, tests/test_tiers.py::test_a_writer_exists_only_when_its_tier_is_on, tests/test_tiers.py::test_approvals_are_bound_to_one_action_unexpired_and_single_use, tests/test_tiers.py::test_the_read_only_backends_cannot_write, tests/test_tiers.py::test_tool_list_is_unchanged_with_defaults_and_tier_tools_need_their_tier, tests/test_tiers.py::test_kube_noobs_own_audit_flags_what_tier_roles_allow, ci:Chart guards (fail-closed NetworkPolicy, fsGroup only off-OpenShift, web flag wired to env)
G38 Signals (alert names, event and ClusterOperator condition reasons, container reasons, admission rules, components, error templates) come only from structured fields or vocabularies checked against the upstream file that emits them; error templates drop times, addresses, ids and pod suffixes so the same error gets the same template; output is sorted and deterministic, and every input is bounded and fast on hostile text tests/test_signals.py::test_alertmanager_payload, tests/test_signal_catalogue.py::test_catalogue_schema, tests/test_signal_catalogue.py::test_openshift_and_upstream_names_both_exist_where_they_differ, tests/test_signals.py::test_events_list_keeps_warnings_and_known_reasons, tests/test_signals.py::test_cluster_operator_conditions, tests/test_signals.py::test_pod_status_reasons, tests/test_signals.py::test_error_templates_are_stable_across_variable_parts, tests/test_signals.py::test_output_is_deterministic_and_sorted, tests/test_signals.py::test_inputs_are_bounded_and_hostile_input_is_cheap, tests/test_policy.py::test_explain_stays_fast_on_hostile_input
G39 Signals rank playbooks once per playbook (a symptom never outranks its specific cause); diagnose, debug and bundle show the signals they read and policy explain shows admission signals; an Alertmanager payload is read as bounded JSON, known issues match each alert's line and summary only, and only an OpenShift 4 release label sets the version; extract_signals is read-only and cites each catalogue entry's source tests/test_signals_everywhere.py::test_an_etcd_alert_payload_ranks_the_etcd_playbook_first_with_known_issues_for_its_version, tests/test_signals_everywhere.py::test_an_explicit_version_wins_over_the_label_and_a_fixed_version_has_no_known_issue, tests/test_signals_everywhere.py::test_payload_reader_is_bounded_and_strips_control_characters, tests/test_signals_everywhere.py::test_only_an_openshift_release_label_sets_the_version, tests/test_signals_everywhere.py::test_signals_break_ties_between_playbooks_with_the_same_text_evidence, tests/test_diagnose_first.py::test_specific_cause_outranks_generic_crashloop, tests/test_signals_everywhere.py::test_debug_and_bundle_show_signals, tests/test_signals_everywhere.py::test_policy_explain_shows_admission_signals, tests/test_signals_everywhere.py::test_extract_signals_tool_is_read_only_strict_and_explains_what_it_found
G40 A memory entry is a reviewed summary, not a dump: prose rejects credentials, raw logs, event rows, stack traces, pipelines and manifests; each command is one line with no shell comment, carries the classifier's label (read-only or needs-approval) and never prints a Secret; a patch is manifests without Secrets or credentials; evidence is a reference only; the file is bounded, has one document, no duplicate keys and is named after its id; the shipped JSON Schema matches the model tests/test_memory_entry.py::test_bad_entries_are_rejected_with_a_reason, tests/test_memory_entry.py::test_commands_carry_the_classifiers_label_and_nothing_else, tests/test_memory_entry.py::test_patches_are_manifests_without_secrets, tests/test_memory_entry.py::test_yaml_level_attacks_are_refused, tests/test_memory_entry.py::test_the_file_name_must_match_the_id, tests/test_memory_entry.py::test_round_trip_is_exact_and_deterministic, tests/test_memory_entry.py::test_the_shipped_json_schema_matches_the_model_and_checks_shape

G121 Bundled YAML is written so that every conformant parser reads it the same way: no colon inside an unquoted scalar in a flow collection, and no key: value pair sitting directly in a [ ] sequence. Both are ambiguous, and PyYAML's own scanner accepts what a stricter libyaml refuses, so a file that parses in CI can break a shipped install. The check reads the scanner's tokens rather than trusting whichever parser is installed, and covers .yaml, .yml and the front matter of knowledge documents, in src/, the plugin copy and examples/. G123 Pasted JSON is refused on its bracket count before it is parsed, so the refusal does not depend on the interpreter: json.loads raises RecursionError up to CPython 3.13 but relies on the C stack from 3.14, where a deep paste overflows the WebAssembly stack as a fatal RangeError and took diagnose and known_issues down in the browser. G124 A must-gather analysis says what it could not see, and a gate can refuse to read a blind run as a clean one: completeness reports the analysers that had data, the files that could not be read and whether the evidence is whole; the text output says so in words; and --require-complete exits 5, distinct from 1 (findings at or above --fail-on) and from 2, 3 and 4 (config, clarification, refused input). G125 The API key the model client sends is only ever the one named for that provider: apiKeyEnv when set, else the provider's own default, and only anthropic has one. G126 A directory of manifests is read one file at a time, so a file nobody can read is named and counted rather than deciding the outcome for every other file: audit -f DIR and gate -f DIR used to concatenate the whole tree before anything parsed it, so one file that was not UTF-8 or not YAML aborted the audit of the rest and reported a line of the combined text, which is no line of any file the user can open. Unreadable files now exit 5, distinct from 1 (findings at or above --fail-on), because a gate that crashed on a NUL byte used to leave 1 and a pipeline read that as policy violations; --skip-unreadable audits the rest and lists them under unreadable in the report, and --fail-on still bites on the files that were read tests/test_red_team_rt3.py::test_a_crash_never_wears_the_exit_code_of_a_policy_failure, tests/test_red_team_rt3.py::test_an_unreadable_file_is_named_not_located_in_a_concatenation, tests/test_red_team_rt3.py::test_one_bad_file_does_not_hide_the_findings_in_the_good_ones, tests/test_red_team_rt3.py::test_skip_unreadable_still_fails_on_severity, tests/test_red_team_rt3.py::test_a_clean_directory_is_unchanged
G127 A reachability verdict evaluates all three network policy tiers, because the two cluster-scoped ones do not narrow the answer, they change it: an AdminNetworkPolicy Deny blocks traffic every NetworkPolicy in the namespace allows, an Allow permits traffic every NetworkPolicy denies, and a BaselineAdminNetworkPolicy decides the traffic no NetworkPolicy selects. Order is ANP (by priority ascending, then rule order, first match wins; Pass ends the tier), then NetworkPolicy -- whose answer is final where it selects the pod -- then BANP, whose no-match is an allow. A manifest the API server would itself refuse (a priority outside 0-1000, a BANP not named default, an action that is not one of the API's) is reported as a mistake rather than evaluated or guessed at, an Allow/Deny above OVN-Kubernetes's priority 99 says that the cluster ignores it, and a nodes or networks peer says it is not evaluated offline. The live check reads both tiers once, cluster-scoped, and a cluster without the CRDs is a note rather than a failure tests/test_netpol_anp.py::test_an_admin_deny_beats_a_networkpolicy_that_allows_everything, tests/test_netpol_anp.py::test_an_admin_allow_beats_a_networkpolicy_that_denies_everything, tests/test_netpol_anp.py::test_pass_hands_the_decision_back_to_the_namespace, tests/test_netpol_anp.py::test_the_lowest_priority_number_decides, tests/test_netpol_anp.py::test_the_baseline_decides_only_what_no_networkpolicy_selects, tests/test_netpol_anp.py::test_a_baseline_not_named_default_is_reported_not_applied, tests/test_netpol_anp.py::test_an_unknown_action_is_reported_and_not_guessed, tests/test_netpol_anp.py::test_the_live_check_reads_the_admin_tiers_too, tests/test_netpol_anp.py::test_a_cluster_that_has_no_admin_tier_api_is_not_an_error, tests/test_netpol_anp.py::test_it_does_not_propose_a_change_that_the_admin_tier_would_still_block, tests/test_safety_contract.py::test_the_chart_read_rules_mirror_the_backends_allowlist
G128 The HTTP server's guards run before the work they protect and on the bytes the work actually reads: a body on /healthz or /readyz is refused with 405 before it is buffered (those are the only paths an unauthenticated caller reaches, and buffering there let anything that could open the port take the pod to its memory limit); the body limit is 1.5 MiB rather than 8 MiB, since every tool argument is capped at 1,000,000 characters so a larger body can never be valid, and each buffered body is copied again and bracket-scanned in pure Python; the JSON nesting guard counts the one decoded string the parser is given, because json.loads sniffs UTF-16 and UTF-32 and a backslash before a NUL byte desynchronised a guard that read a UTF-8 decoding of the same bytes; two Authorization headers are refused rather than resolved last-wins; and the content-free audit line truncates argument names, which arrive unvalidated from the caller on the REST path tests/test_red_team_rt4.py::test_an_unauthenticated_caller_cannot_make_the_server_buffer_a_body, tests/test_red_team_rt4.py::test_the_body_limit_is_not_larger_than_a_valid_request, tests/test_red_team_rt4.py::test_the_guard_counted_a_different_string_from_the_one_the_parser_read, tests/test_red_team_rt4.py::test_the_nesting_guard_reads_the_same_bytes_the_parser_does, tests/test_red_team_rt4.py::test_a_valid_utf16_body_is_still_understood, tests/test_red_team_rt4.py::test_a_duplicate_authorization_header_is_refused, tests/test_red_team_rt4.py::test_the_audit_line_does_not_log_what_the_caller_chose_to_send
G129 The knowledge index is cached on the content that feeds it, not on the file that holds it: learning.json contributes the team's confirmed notes as searchable chunks, but almost every tool call rewrites it to bump a counter, so keying the cache on its size and mtime rebuilt the whole index on nearly every request -- about fourteen times slower on the shared HTTP server. The key covers the notes themselves, so a new note is searchable at once, a counter costs nothing, and a file that cannot be read as notes falls back to its stat rather than serving a stale index tests/test_red_team_rt4.py::test_the_knowledge_store_is_not_rebuilt_because_a_counter_moved
G130 A burst of connections that send nothing starves the HTTP server's pool -- uvicorn counts connections towards limit_concurrency and nothing reclaims one that never sent a request, so every request including the kubelet's probes answers 503 until they close (measured: 70 idle sockets, still 503 after 16s). kube-noob cannot close that in process, so what it owns stays consistent instead: buffered-body memory is the concurrency limit times the body limit and has to leave the container room to run (64 x 1.5 MiB against 512Mi, where it used to be 64 x 8 MiB -- exactly the ceiling the kernel kills at); the liveness probe is four times more forgiving than readiness, so sustained overload sheds traffic rather than restarting a pod whose restart frees no connection; and SECURITY.md says the port belongs behind a proxy that enforces a header-read timeout tests/test_red_team_rt6.py::test_the_concurrency_limit_and_the_body_limit_stay_consistent, tests/test_red_team_rt6.py::test_overload_sheds_traffic_rather_than_restarting_the_pod, tests/test_red_team_rt6.py::test_the_security_policy_states_the_limitation
G131 The release job attaches its assets whether or not the release already exists. gh release create is an error on an existing release, and a maintainer creating one by hand -- to write their own notes, or because a tag push looked like it did nothing -- is ordinary: on v0.7.1 it failed github-release and skipped videos and site behind it, so the release carried no wheel, no chart and no videos, and kube-noob.com was never told the version had changed, while PyPI, the chart repository, the MCP registry and Homebrew had all published. The step branches on gh release view and uploads with --clobber, so a re-run replaces an asset instead of failing on it tests/test_distribution.py::test_the_release_job_survives_a_release_the_maintainer_made_first
G132 The plugin declares exactly the dependencies the bundled server imports, checked both ways: a module imported but not declared is an ImportError on the user's first call, because uv runs the server --frozen --exact --isolated and nothing ambient fills the gap; a dependency declared but never imported is a package the user installs for nothing and a reviewer has to account for. The plugin had declared httpx and uvicorn, which belong to the web client and the HTTP service and ship in neither, and left anyio, mcp-types and pydantic-core to arrive through another package's requirements although it imports all three directly. Dropping the two removed httpx, httpcore and certifi from the resolved tree, so the installed set holds no HTTP client at all. The launcher stays the vendored one and the README says why, rather than promising a switch to a published package that was reverted before the release tests/test_plugin.py::test_the_plugin_declares_what_it_imports_and_imports_what_it_declares, tests/test_plugin.py::test_lock_pins_every_dependency_with_registry_and_hashes, tests/test_plugin.py::test_mcp_config_runs_the_bundled_source_from_the_lock
G133 The audit refuses what it cannot audit instead of scoring it. Given a Helm values file it used to read the whole document as one unnamed object, report that apiVersion was missing, and return a score -- 90 out of 100 for a file whose images were :latest and whose securityContext and resources were empty. A score says the rules were applied, so a score on a file the rules never saw is a wrong answer rather than a thin one. G188 Two defects a manifest proves and kube-noob did not report. G189 An audit rule that re-implements what the reach engine already does will disagree with it. KN-N013 shipped with its own port and policyType handling and a red team found six wrong answers in it: TCP-only 53 cleared the finding though protocol defaults to TCP and a resolver queries UDP first (glibc falls back only on a truncated answer, musl not at all); an omitted policyTypes both hid a real stranding and reported a working allowance, because the API adds Egress whenever egress rules exist; {port: 1, endPort: 1024} was read as port 1; a named port such as CoreDNS's dns was reported though the DNS pod is never in the bundle and the manifest cannot prove stranding; and a hostNetwork pod was reported though NetworkPolicy does not govern it. The fix is to use netpol.policy_types() and the same port semantics as _port_matches, not a second implementation. G190 A finding's identity must survive edits that did not change the finding, and it is NOT the SARIF fingerprint. Finding.fingerprint() hashes the rule, the object and a NAME-KEYED locator: containers[name=api].securityContext, not containers[0]. An index is a position and positions move, so inserting a sidecar at the front renamed every finding on every other container and a baseline would report N fixed and N new for a change that fixed nothing -- the team then runs baseline update to get CI green and the update absorbs whatever real regression landed that week. The message is excluded because several rules embed a count or a sorted list in it (KN-N012 counts the namespace's pod templates), the uri because a move or a run from a subdirectory rewrites it, docIndex because adding a file shifts every later one, and severity because rules override it per finding. G197 A compliance format is a contract, and three of its rules are not guessable -- each was read off NIST's published schema and catalogue, and each would have produced a document that looks right and is wrong. A finding may not target a control: finding-target.type is an enum of exactly statement-id and objective-id, so an OSCAL finding targets the control's objective id, ac-6.10_obj, which the catalogue defines. Control ids are catalogue ids, not the labels: AC-6(10) becomes ac-6.10, and parentheses are not legal in OSCAL's TokenDatatype at all, so emitting the label yields a document that cannot validate. All 28 of kube-noob's controls and their _obj ids were confirmed present in NIST_SP-800-53_rev5_catalog.json (catalogue 5.2.0), and the mapping is pinned in a fixture because the catalogue is 10 MB. Validation is ajv, not Python's jsonschema: TokenDatatype is ^(\p{L}|_)(\p{L}|\p{N}|[.\-_])*$ and Python's re cannot compile a Unicode property escape, so jsonschema raises PatternError the moment validation reaches a token -- including on a document whose only fault is an illegal control id. (This entry first said it SKIPS the pattern and would call an invalid document valid. A red team disproved that: it fails loudly, not quietly. The conclusion was right for the wrong reason, and test_python_jsonschema_cannot_validate_this_schema_at_all now pins the real behaviour so the claim cannot drift back.) CI installs ajv and then asserts the test did not SKIP, because a skipped check is the same absence-read-as-pass this project keeps fixing. The honesty rules carry over from G191: satisfied is emitted only where the coverage record says that control's rules ran and found nothing, a control nothing evaluated is excluded rather than passed, with no coverage record nothing is satisfied at all, an exception is not-satisfied with reason other rather than a pass, and the required import-ap points at a fragment whose remarks say no plan was imported rather than naming one that does not exist tests/test_oscal.py::test_a_finding_targets_an_objective_because_the_schema_forbids_a_control, tests/test_oscal.py::test_every_control_resolves_to_the_pinned_nist_catalogue_id, tests/test_oscal.py::test_no_emitted_id_would_break_the_token_pattern, tests/test_oscal.py::test_python_jsonschema_cannot_validate_this_schema_at_all, tests/test_oscal.py::test_the_document_validates_against_nists_published_schema, tests/test_oscal.py::test_a_control_no_rule_evaluated_is_excluded_and_never_satisfied, tests/test_oscal.py::test_without_a_coverage_record_nothing_is_reported_satisfied, tests/test_oscal.py::test_the_missing_assessment_plan_is_stated_rather_than_faked, tests/test_oscal.py::test_the_document_is_reproducible_byte_for_byte
G198 The same absent field means different things on different platforms, and a rule that ignores that reports a platform's design as a defect. Pod Security admission is configured by namespace labels (pod-security.kubernetes.io/enforce, upstream's own constant), and the obvious rule -- "flag a namespace with no enforce label" -- is WRONG on OpenShift: the label syncer sets warn and audit from the SCCs a namespace's service accounts can use and deliberately not enforce, because OpenShift enforces the privileged profile globally and leaves real enforcement to SCCs. So KN-S032 is silent on the OpenShift family for an absent label. It reports an explicit enforce: privileged on every platform, because that is a decision rather than a default. And on vanilla Kubernetes it does NOT say the namespace enforces nothing: the level is whatever the cluster's PodSecurity AdmissionConfiguration default is, and no manifest shows that, so the finding says the answer is not in the repository -- the same three-valued discipline the policy commands use. baseline and restricted are judgements about the workload, not defects, so neither is reported. The namespaces the platform owns are skipped by reusing admission.SYSTEM_NAMESPACES rather than writing a second list of the same names (G189), plus openshift-* and default, for which Pod Security sync is permanently off. The repository's own example namespaces were labelled in the same change, so the examples hold to what the tool audits for; a check confirmed the findings for those files are byte-identical before and after, because no rule reads those labels yet tests/test_pod_security.py::test_no_label_is_silent_on_openshift_because_the_syncer_sets_warn_and_audit_only, tests/test_pod_security.py::test_no_label_is_reported_on_kubernetes_and_names_the_cluster_default, tests/test_pod_security.py::test_privileged_is_reported_as_the_no_restrictions_level, tests/test_pod_security.py::test_privileged_is_reported_on_openshift_too, tests/test_pod_security.py::test_baseline_and_restricted_are_judgements_not_defects, tests/test_pod_security.py::test_the_namespaces_the_platform_owns_are_skipped, tests/test_pod_security.py::test_a_workload_without_its_namespace_object_is_not_a_finding
G199 A rule that FIRED must never be counted clean because the caller filtered its finding out of this view. --min-severity high filters report.findings but not the record of which rules ran, and controlSummary was built from the filtered findings plus the unfiltered evaluated set -- so every rule whose finding was withheld fell into clean. CP-10 went from "3 findings, 6 clean" to "0 findings, 9 clean", and the OSCAL document then reported all 28 controls satisfied for a bundle with a privileged container, a root user and a plaintext password, at exit 0. This is the same absence-read-as-pass as G191, reintroduced by a flag: G191 fixed "nothing ran" looking like "nothing found", and the filter recreated it as "found but hidden" looking like "nothing found". The report now carries fired_rules, recorded BEFORE the filter, and those rules are counted as withheld -- not a finding in this view, and emphatically not clean. The invariant the tests hold is that clean does not depend on --min-severity at all, that findings + excepted + clean + withheld + notEvaluated accounts for every rule of a control at every filter level, and that the OSCAL verdict per control is identical at every filter level. A withheld control is not-satisfied with reason other, never a pass. G200 A red team on the OSCAL emitter found five more defects, and they fall into two shapes. An identity must cover everything that changes what it identifies. The uuid was keyed on platform, version and resource names, so a default run and a --min-severity critical run -- one reporting fifteen controls not-satisfied, the other none -- produced the same assessment-results uuid, the same result uuid, and fifteen finding uuids that flipped verdict without changing identity. The key now includes every finding's fingerprint, every control's buckets and the imported plan. Relatedly the observation list was ordered by (severity, docIndex, line), so listing the same two files in the other order gave the same uuids and different bytes; it is sorted by uuid now. And an observation uuid keyed on the finding fingerprint alone collided for two containers failing one pod-level check -- the fingerprint excludes the message by design (G190), which is right for a finding's cross-run identity and wrong for "a thing that was seen", so the message joins the observation key and a consumer resolving related-observations can reach both. Anything a manifest supplies can violate a datatype. property.value is StringDatatype (^\S(.*\S)?$), and a YAML folded scalar yields Deployment/shop/orders-api\n while the typo KN-V007 exists to report yields the path spec.bad field -- both produced documents no OSCAL tool will ingest, at exit 1, so a pipeline read it as business as usual. G205 When a rule you added reports your OWN artifact, the finding is the first evidence that the rule works -- and weakening it so your artifact passes is the failure this project keeps correcting in other people's reasoning. G207 Evaluating a policy the cluster's CNI ignores is not a cautious answer, it is a wrong one. policy reach evaluates AdminNetworkPolicy and BaselineAdminNetworkPolicy with OVN-Kubernetes semantics, and Calico removed support for both in 3.32 in favour of ClusterNetworkPolicy: "Calico does not enforce them from 3.32 onward, so they stop taking effect silently" (Tigera upgrade notes, retrieved 2026-11-05). On such a cluster the tier kube-noob trusted FIRST is the one doing nothing, so a Deny it reported was a deny that was not happening and an Allow was permission nobody had granted -- and both read as a confident verdict. --cni NAME[:VERSION] fixes it with three answers rather than two, because the two-valued version is where the trap is. enforced (the upstream network-policy-api implementations page lists OVN-Kubernetes, Antrea, Kube-OVN, kube-network-policies and Calico 3.29-3.31) behaves as before. not-enforced drops the tier, lets the NetworkPolicies answer -- which can be the OPPOSITE verdict -- and reports each policy as inertPolicies with the reason and the replacement kind, printed on the line under the verdict because a finding only the JSON carries is one the operator does not read. unknown (Cilium, which that page lists with a tracking issue and no stated support) drops the tier AND makes the verdict undetermined, because evaluating it assumes enforcement and ignoring it assumes the reverse; it does not borrow the tier_unreadable wording, since the policy was read and the doubt is about the CNI. Three constraints the design turns on: a CNI absent from the table is unknown, never enforced, so the table never guesses in its owner's favour; Calico with no version is unknown too, because for that CNI the version is the whole answer; and an unknown CNI with nothing in that tier answers normally, since the doubt is about the tier and not about the cluster. The default, with no flag, is unchanged -- an opt-in, so no existing answer moves under anyone. Not built in policy migrate-check as the roadmap guessed: that command is a Kyverno rule-by-rule lint, and the wrong answer was coming out of policy reach tests/test_cni_admin_tier.py::test_the_table_answers_only_where_upstream_does, tests/test_cni_admin_tier.py::test_every_entry_names_where_it_comes_from, tests/test_cni_admin_tier.py::test_an_admin_deny_a_cni_ignores_does_not_deny, tests/test_cni_admin_tier.py::test_an_admin_allow_a_cni_ignores_does_not_allow, tests/test_cni_admin_tier.py::test_a_baseline_a_cni_ignores_leaves_the_traffic_allowed, tests/test_cni_admin_tier.py::test_an_unknown_cni_makes_the_answer_undetermined_rather_than_either, tests/test_cni_admin_tier.py::test_an_unknown_cni_with_no_admin_policy_answers_normally, tests/test_cni_admin_tier.py::test_no_cni_keeps_the_documented_ovn_kubernetes_semantics, tests/test_cni_admin_tier.py::test_the_flag_reaches_the_evaluator_and_the_terminal, tests/test_cni_admin_tier.py::test_a_bad_flag_value_is_refused_by_the_schema
G208 A rule can only be as strong as the thing its input proves, and the numeric case proves less than the named one. KN-V012 reports a NAMED targetPort no selected container declares, at HIGH, because a name must resolve to a declared port or the Service gets no endpoints at all. KN-V023 reports the numeric case and cannot borrow any of that: containerPort is informational, so a Service pointing at 8080 works whenever a container listens on 8080, declared or not. A rule that reported every undeclared numeric port would fire on correct repositories -- the absence-read-as-DEFECT failure, which is the one this project keeps having to resist while hunting the absence-read-as-pass one. So the rule narrows to the case where there IS evidence: these containers declare ports, and the Service points outside that set, so one of the two is wrong. Where the containers declare no ports, there is no signal and the rule is silent; it is LOW; and the finding says Kubernetes does not require the declaration, because a LOW that reads like a broken Service sends someone to fix working YAML. Two details carry the same stance: an omitted targetPort IS checked, because it defaults to port -- treating its absence as safe would be the original defect in the other direction -- and a port declared only on an init container does not clear a target, because a Service never reaches one. G211 A job timeout is not a diagnosis. The videos job was cancelled at 30m19s on a run whose other 19 checks were green and whose own steps reported no failure, so the only thing the 30 minutes said was "something took too long". Reproducing it locally with the pinned Playwright 1.63.0 measured the whole render at about 90 seconds -- voiceover 7-17s per video, capture.js --check 6-9s, the stills 5-7s, the walkthrough and thumbnail 11s, 23 stills out -- which rules out the render and points at the install step, where playwright install --with-deps runs apt-get and an apt prompt on a runner with no tty waits forever. So: DEBIAN_FRONTEND=noninteractive with sudo -E (plain sudo drops the environment, which would have made the fix a no-op), and a timeout-minutes on every step, so the next hang fails with its own name instead of eating the budget. The test that pins it checks the budgets SUM under the job's own limit too, and caught the first attempt at 32 against 30: three bounded steps that can still only die at the job boundary are not bounded. The bounded step then fired, which is the point, and a 12-minute step that dies at its own limit still only says "something in here hangs". So it is one command per step now: pip, apt, npm ci, the browser download. Measured off the runner, pip is 20s and npm ci is 3s, which leaves the two commands that cannot be measured here -- and both apt hangs are now closed rather than guessed between. DEBIAN_FRONTEND answers the prompt-with-no-tty case; -o DPkg::Lock::Timeout=180 answers the dpkg lock unattended-upgrades holds, where apt's default is to wait with no limit at all. And --with-deps is gone: it runs apt a SECOND time inside Playwright's own sudo, where neither of those reaches it. G213 When the fact is readable, read it; the flag is for when it is not. --cni (G207) was the caller's claim, and on --live kube-noob is already talking to the cluster -- so it now reads Calico's own version from clusterinformations.crd.projectcalico.org, whose spec.calicoVersion is "set automatically by calico/node" (upstream CRD, projectcalico/calico, retrieved 2026-11-05; group, version v1, scope Cluster and plural taken from that file rather than guessed, the G184 lesson). Without it, policy reach --live on a Calico 3.32 cluster kept OVN-Kubernetes semantics and reported a deny that is not happening, on the one path where the operator cannot read the policies for themselves. Four reads deliberately yield nothing rather than a guess: the API saying it does not serve the CRD (no Calico, and no note either -- nothing changed); a 403, any other failed read or a body that is not a list of objects (since G279: the CNI is unknown and every admin-tier reading a CNI could have is compared, undetermined unless all agree); the CRD served but empty, an object with no calicoVersion or one that does not parse (since G279: Calico of unknown version, G209's unknown, not "no Calico"); and a variant other than open-source Calico, whose versions run on their own schedule (unknown enforcement). An explicit --cni wins over the read, because a caller who passes it knows something a forbidden read cannot see. And writing the tests reversed a decision from G209. That entry stopped reading a tier the CNI ignores, to keep a 403 on it from withholding the verdict -- but not reading it also loses the inert finding, which is the most valuable thing in the output: "this AdminNetworkPolicy is in your cluster and decides nothing" needs the policy in hand. The tier is read again, and only the FAILURE of that read is dismissed. G215 A limit that drops output silently has to be spent on saying so. gate --sarif wrote SARIF and action.yml ran the gate, but nothing turned a finding into an annotation, so a finding landed in a log a developer had to go looking for. GitHub's own caps shape the fix: "10 warning annotations, 10 error annotations, and 10 notice annotations per step" and "50 annotations per job" (actions/toolkit, docs/problem-matchers.md, retrieved 2026-11-05). One annotation per finding would mean the eleventh is dropped with no message, and a developer who sees ten annotations reasonably concludes there are ten problems -- a count that stops being true, which is this repository's most-repeated defect. So nine findings are annotated and the tenth slot of each kind carries the number that is not shown and points at the SARIF and the job summary. An annotation is an error when it is a finding the gate failed on and a warning otherwise, which is not the same as being at or above --fail-on: the first version read the severity, and G216 records the two wrong verdicts that produced. The escaping follows the toolkit's rules, and the properties escape a comma and a colon pair while the message does not need to -- the message is the rest of the line. In the action the stream is captured once and split, because the workflow commands and the JSON report both come out on stdout and leaving the :: lines in a .json file would hand the next person a file that does not parse tests/test_github_annotations.py::test_a_finding_at_or_above_the_threshold_is_an_error_annotation, tests/test_github_annotations.py::test_a_finding_below_the_threshold_is_a_warning, tests/test_github_annotations.py::test_the_message_is_one_line_and_escaped, tests/test_github_annotations.py::test_a_finding_with_no_file_still_annotates, tests/test_github_annotations.py::test_the_last_annotation_says_how_many_were_not_shown, tests/test_github_annotations.py::test_both_kinds_are_capped_independently, tests/test_github_annotations.py::test_never_as_a_threshold_makes_every_finding_a_warning, tests/test_github_annotations.py::test_the_total_stays_under_githubs_per_job_cap, tests/test_github_annotations.py::test_the_cli_prints_them_and_keeps_the_exit_code, tests/test_github_annotations.py::test_the_action_emits_them, tests/test_github_annotations.py::test_a_json_report_is_unchanged_by_the_flag
G216 Red-teaming G214 and G215 found seven defects, and two of them were wrong verdicts -- the same class the two rows were written to close. (1) snapshot() filtered the collected objects down to the kinds it tracks before the rules saw them, so a bundle holding a ResourceQuota was audited as if it had none and KN-C016 fired on an absence that was not there; the rules now see every object with a kind, and tracking and auditing are separate questions. (2) A snapshot whose reads were refused counted the rules that never ran as clean -- a forbidden roles read published "AC-6 16 clean". The engine knows which rules fire on an absence but not which kind each rule reads, so no clean on a partial snapshot can be trusted: standing is now withheld entirely, with the reason, whenever notRead is non-empty, and the audit runs with partial_bundle=True so absence rules decline rather than fire. (3) github_annotations read the severity, so an unfixable critical CVE -- above --fail-on, not a gate failure -- was an ::error on a green step; the level now comes from a failsGate flag set at the three places the gate decides. G222 The first HTML kube-noob has ever produced, and the rules that make it trustworthy rather than merely prettier (U1). Until now --format was text, json or yaml and the only HTML the code touched was web content it read; the audience for per-control standing and the KSI crosswalk is a compliance reviewer, and what a reviewer got was terminal text they cannot forward or JSON they will not read. Three constraints, each one a test rather than an intention. The page fetches nothing: no stylesheet, script, font or image, verified by parsing the output for every attribute that makes a browser load something and again by rendering it in Chromium with the request log checked. The caveats come before the findings, because a reader who meets an empty findings table first has already concluded -- audited: false, assumed, notCheckedBeyond, an unreadable file, a withheld standing and the shows/does-not-show pair all survive, since an HTML report that drops them is G221 in a third format. And nothing from a manifest is ever markup: this is the one place in the product where someone else's resource names become a document a third person opens, so every value is escaped and a Deployment named <script>alert(1)</script> renders as visible, inert text. The backtick-to-code-span conversion escapes first and only then looks for backticks, so the only markup it can find is markup this code wrote. Two defects the first render showed that no test had: the byline read "by kube-noob 2026.11.02.01" because the version fell back to the FedRAMP dataset's -- a false claim about the tool in the first line of a document written for someone checking claims -- and an unevidenced indicator's explanatory note sat in the Findings column, which is the one column on that page a reader must not misread. G226 The first honest check in the logging family, and the discipline that made it smaller than it looked. Two outside reviews put audit logging and SIEM at the top of kube-noob's missing coverage and proposed about twelve rules each. Eleven of the twelve need credentials for a system this project holds none for, so they could only ever answer "not evaluated": coverage theatre. The twelfth is real -- OpenShift keeps audit settings in a cluster-scoped APIServer singleton that ordinary read-only RBAC can read -- and KN-S035 reads it. Verified against openshift/api config/v1/types_apiserver.go before a line was written: cluster-scoped, named cluster, spec.audit.profile one of Default, WriteRequestBodies, AllRequestBodies or None, and customRules a per-group list evaluated top to bottom whose first match takes precedence over the top-level profile. That last clause is the finding worth having: a cluster reporting profile: Default can log nothing for a named group, so it looks audited and is not -- this repository's own defect class, sitting in someone else's API. What happened next is the part to remember. The rule brought AU-2 and AU-12 into the control catalogue, and the crosswalk promptly moved THREE indicators off the unevidenced list: KSI-MLA-LET, KSI-MLA-OSM and KSI-MLA-RVL. It has business claiming exactly one. LET asks which event types are logged, which is the rule's subject; OSM asks whether a SIEM is operated and RVL whether anyone reads the logs, and both arrive only because FedRAMP and kube-noob both name au-2. G227 CodeQL was wrong about the category and right about the code. It flagged api_version.startswith("config.openshift.io/") in KN-S035 as incomplete URL sanitization. The framing does not hold -- apiVersion is a Kubernetes group/version string, not a URL -- and the alert was not exploitable either, because the trailing slash makes it a true prefix: config.openshift.io.attacker.example/v1 never matched it. The temptation was therefore to dismiss it. But a prefix match is the wrong tool for an API group, which is a field with a separator and an exact value, so the check now splits on / and compares the group. Same behaviour for every input that matters, clearer intent, and the alert goes away because the construct it pattern-matched on is gone rather than suppressed. A test pins both halves -- a look-alike group decides nothing, and any served version of the real group is read -- so the property survives the next person who finds the split verbose. The general lesson: a scanner's classification can be wrong while its finger is on something worth changing, and "the alert is a false positive" is an argument about the label, not about the line tests/test_audit_profile.py::test_only_the_real_api_group_is_read
G228 A release step that imports the package needs the package's dependencies, and a second setup-python silently takes them away. v0.8.0's release failed at krew_manifest.py --check with No module named 'yaml'. Nothing was published -- PyPI returned 404 for 0.8.0 -- so the version survived, which is the only reason this is a CI bug and not a burned release number. The cause is an ordering nobody would spot by reading: build-dist calls setup-python twice, once to build and once to match the interpreter inside the pinned PyPI publish action, and every step after that second switch runs on a fresh Python with no project dependencies. It had never mattered, because no step after the switch imported kube_noob -- until #42 added a rule count to the krew plugin's description, which pulls in yamlio and so PyYAML. The last several releases passing is what made it invisible: the import arrived in a commit whose own CI was entirely green, because ci.yml installs the package and release.yml is the only workflow that does not. Fixed by installing the same hash-locked requirements.lock the action itself installs, so the scripts run against the dependency set that ships rather than whatever resolves on the day. Verified both directions in clean virtual environments before pushing: bare interpreter reproduces the ModuleNotFoundError, locked install passes both --checks. The guard asserts the ordering rather than the symptom -- after the last interpreter switch, no step may import the package before a step has installed its dependencies -- because the next script added to that job will have the same problem and a test for "yaml is importable" would not have caught it tests/test_distribution.py::test_the_release_installs_dependencies_before_importing_the_package
G229 The pod specs that are not at spec.template.spec, and the blind spot that read as clean. A KServe InferenceService running a privileged, root container with hostPath: / and a mutable tag produced 14 findings as a Deployment and zero as an InferenceService -- not "not evaluated", clean. Every pod-level rule in the catalogue, and the whole attack-path scan, read pod specs only from the nine ordinary workload paths, so an operator CRD that embeds its own pod spec was invisible to all of them: the exact defect class G219-G228 exist to prevent, this time caused by a reader's silence rather than a rule's verdict. Two registries fix it, and the discipline is in how they were filled. Every path was read out of the published CRD schema on 2026-11-06, not inferred from a tutorial: KServe's predictor, transformer and explainer are each a bare inline PodSpec (the schema carries all eleven PodSpec fields, hostNetwork, hostPID and hostIPC among them) from config/crd/full/serving.kserve.io_inferenceservices.yaml, and KubeRay's headGroupSpec.template and each workerGroupSpecs[].template are ordinary PodTemplateSpecs, with RayService wrapping them under rayClusterConfig and RayJob under rayClusterSpec. A second registry pins the API group of each kind, so a RayCluster in someone else's group is left alone (G227) -- while a kind with no apiVersion is read, because both live collectors set only kind and API list items carry no apiVersion of their own, so refusing an absent group would have restored the blind spot for every --live scan. The attack-path scan can score such a component's node and API capabilities but often cannot trace an entry edge to it, which it says in limitations naming the component -- silence there would have read as "not exposed", the same failure one level up. And the fix is deliberately smaller than the problem: four kinds with verified schemas, not every CRD that might carry a pod, because a guessed path is a wrong finding and a wrong finding is worse than a known gap. Two claims in the first version of this row were wrong and are corrected by G231: that reading containers was enough for a KServe component, and that its labels() should return nothing. Both were reasoned from the shape of the API instead of from its published schema and its controller's source, which is the thing this row says not to do tests/test_embedded_podspecs.py::test_an_inference_service_is_audited_not_passed, tests/test_embedded_podspecs.py::test_every_pod_level_rule_that_fires_on_a_deployment_fires_on_a_ray_cluster, tests/test_embedded_podspecs.py::test_a_same_named_kind_from_another_group_is_not_read, tests/test_embedded_podspecs.py::test_a_kind_with_no_api_version_is_read_because_the_live_collectors_drop_it, tests/test_embedded_podspecs.py::test_a_kserve_component_carries_the_labels_the_controller_actually_sets, tests/test_embedded_podspecs.py::test_the_scan_reaches_a_ray_head_and_every_worker_group, tests/test_embedded_podspecs.py::test_an_unmatchable_component_is_named_as_undetermined_not_left_silent, tests/test_embedded_podspecs.py::test_every_embedded_kind_is_in_the_read_only_allowlist

G231 Red-teaming the fix found the same defect one level further down, twice, plus two regressions the fix itself introduced. G229 taught the readers four CRDs and claimed the blind spot closed. An adversarial pass over it, with the published schemas downloaded and walked rather than remembered, found that the fix missed the form almost every KServe manifest in the world uses: a predictor is written model: {modelFormat: ..., storageUri: ...}, and the schema makes model -- and the ten framework shorthands sklearn, xgboost, tensorflow, pytorch, triton, onnx, huggingface, pmml, lightgbm, paddle, plus explainer.art -- each a full v1.Container, a FIELD of the component rather than an entry in its containers list. Reading only containers gave a privileged, root, hostPath: / predictor 2 findings where the identical spec as a list gave 11, and the G229 bookkeeping now recorded every container rule as having evaluated it. The audit and the scan read containers through different functions, so the same input also scored no node capability at all. Three more unread pod specs came out of the same walk: KServe predictor.workerSpec and canary[].predictor, and KubeRay's submitterPodTemplate and its autoscalerOptions, a container the operator injects into the head pod. The labels decision was wrong in the opposite direction, and that one shipped as a false positive. G229 returned {} for a KServe component on the theory that the object's labels are not the pod's. KServe's own buildObjectMeta says they are: the pod's labels are the union of the ServingRuntime's, the object's, the component's labels, and serving.kserve.io/inferenceservice plus component. So the manifest-known set is a SUBSET of the real set, and {} merged "no labels" with "labels unknown" -- which made KN-V011 and KN-N012 tell a reader that a correct Service has no endpoints and a correct NetworkPolicy protects nothing. The fix is selector_decides, three-valued: a positive match on a subset stays a match on the whole, because matchLabels, In and Exists only ever require a label to be PRESENT, so True is trustworthy; a match resting on NotIn or DoesNotExist is undecided, because an unseen label falsifies it; and a non-match is undecided unless a label we can actually see contradicts the selector. Both rules now decline rather than fire, and declining removes them from the evaluated set, so "did not decide" cannot be published as "checked and clean". The component ref broke a consumer: vuln threat keys objects by object_ref, found no RayCluster/ns/r for the key RayCluster/ns/r[headGroupSpec], and reported zero applications and zero threats for a Ray head the scan had just called internet-reachable. Reach carries the carrying object's ref as a field now, so the next consumer cannot get the parsing wrong. And four smaller ones, each the same shape: sorted() over a YAML mapping with an int key (1:) raised TypeError out of the audit, the scan and the admission webhook; KN-N001 was scoped to LONG_RUNNING_KINDS, so a namespace running only KServe or Ray was never asked for a NetworkPolicy and the rule was recorded as evaluated-clean on it; a same-named kind from a foreign group was refused in silence, where a Deployment with a made-up group gets KN-V002; and the new per-scan caveat reached the JSON reader only, which is G221 verbatim. The limitation's own predicate was wrong twice over -- "has no labels" told a plain label-less Pod that an operator sets its labels (false), and missed a labelled Ray head that is still unreachable from the manifest -- and its first wording said the capabilities were "scored above" when with no path nothing about them is printed above at all; it names them inline instead. The lesson is not that the reviewers were clever. It is that G229 was written from the shape of an API rather than from its published schema and its controller's source, in exactly the way G229's own row warns against, and that a change which makes a reader see more is also a change that makes every consumer of that reader's output wrong in a new way tests/test_embedded_podspecs.py::test_the_model_form_scores_the_same_as_the_containers_form, tests/test_embedded_podspecs.py::test_the_scan_scores_the_model_form_too, tests/test_embedded_podspecs.py::test_every_framework_shorthand_is_a_container, tests/test_embedded_podspecs.py::test_an_injected_autoscaler_container_is_audited, tests/test_embedded_podspecs.py::test_a_ray_job_submitter_pod_is_audited, tests/test_embedded_podspecs.py::test_a_correct_network_policy_is_not_called_useless, tests/test_embedded_podspecs.py::test_a_selector_that_contradicts_a_known_label_still_fires, tests/test_embedded_podspecs.py::test_a_declining_rule_is_not_recorded_as_evaluated, tests/test_embedded_podspecs.py::test_the_threat_model_still_sees_an_embedded_workload, tests/test_embedded_podspecs.py::test_a_mapping_with_mixed_key_types_does_not_crash_the_audit, tests/test_embedded_podspecs.py::test_a_namespace_of_only_serving_workloads_still_needs_a_network_policy, tests/test_embedded_podspecs.py::test_a_refused_api_group_is_said_out_loud, tests/test_embedded_podspecs.py::test_a_plain_pod_without_labels_is_not_told_an_operator_sets_them, tests/test_embedded_podspecs.py::test_a_kserve_component_carries_the_labels_the_controller_actually_sets
G232 There are more workload CRDs than four, and a pod spec in a kind with no reader said nothing at all. G229 and G231 taught the readers KServe and KubeRay. A sweep of seventeen other widely deployed CRDs -- Argo Rollout, Knative Service, Kubeflow Notebook, PyTorchJob/TFJob/MPIJob, Argo Workflow, Tekton, Volcano Job, JobSet, KEDA ScaledJob, Spark, Flink, Seldon, ECK, Prometheus, core PodTemplate -- found the same privileged, hostPath: / pod spec producing zero findings in every one of them, and posture scan reporting 0 workloads for a file of Argo Rollouts, which is indistinguishable from a clean input. The tempting fix is to add seventeen registry entries. That is the wrong fix twice over: the paths would be written from how each API looks rather than from its published schema, which is precisely the mistake G231 exists to record, and the eighteenth CRD would be silent again anyway. The fix is to detect the SHAPE and report the gap: a mapping holding a containers list with at least one image is a pod spec in all but name, wherever it sits, and an object of an unreadable kind that contains one is reported as "N pod spec(s) are in kinds kube-noob has no reader for, so their containers were NOT audited", with the object and the path. The scan records the same as a notRead entry, because its kind filter drops such objects before anything else sees them. Narrowness is the whole design: containers whose entries have no image, a containers list of strings, and a ConfigMap whose VALUE is the word all stay quiet, and a kind the registries DO read is never also flagged, because there the registry is the claim and a second message would contradict it. One lesson from building it, worth more than the feature: the first draft capped its own walk at 24 levels while the loader accepts 100, so a pod spec nested deeper would have gone unmentioned -- the exact silent miss the detector exists to end, reintroduced inside the detector. It inherits the loader's depth limit now, and a test nests a pod spec past the old cap tests/test_embedded_podspecs.py::test_a_pod_spec_in_a_kind_with_no_reader_is_reported_not_passed, tests/test_embedded_podspecs.py::test_a_training_job_reports_every_replica_spec_it_could_not_read, tests/test_embedded_podspecs.py::test_an_object_that_merely_mentions_containers_is_not_accused, tests/test_embedded_podspecs.py::test_a_kind_the_registries_do_read_is_not_also_flagged, tests/test_embedded_podspecs.py::test_the_scan_says_it_dropped_a_workload_crd_rather_than_reporting_none, tests/test_embedded_podspecs.py::test_the_detector_looks_as_deep_as_the_loader_accepts
G233 A kind name is unique only inside its group, and an exposure a CRD declares is still an exposure. Two findings from the same sweep, opposite in direction. Use v1" -- as a CRITICAL, which fails a gate on a valid manifest. The same for a Volcano Job, and waiting for anyone's Build, Task, Pipeline or Workflow. A wrong CRITICAL costs more trust than a missed finding, and the fix is to say what the table is actually authoritative about: KNOWN_API_GROUPS, the groups the catalogue covers.
