G320 A failure that cannot name its own cause costs more than the failure. The e2e install handler dumped events only, so a crash-looping init container reported that it was backing off and never what it printed; the e2e-kind (OpenShift 4.22, Kubernetes 1.35) leg failed this way on main and on three branches with nothing in the log to act on, while 4.20, 4.21 and Kubernetes 1.37 passed. Events describe the kubelet's view, logs describe the program's. scripts/ci_pod_failure.sh now prints both, for every container of every pod, init containers first and --previous beside the current attempt, and both call sites use the one script rather than two copies of a one-line handler. ci:e2e-kind and ci:policy-kind call scripts/ci_pod_failure.sh on an install that does not become ready.
G319 A permission tightened on files silently tightened the directory holding them. Containerfile.db's COPY --chmod=0444 into /kube-noob-db/ left that directory at 0444 under a newer BuildKit, which creates the destination for a multi-source copy. Without the execute bit nothing non-root could traverse it, so the archive was unreadable to the image's own user (1001) and to the chart's init container, and the only visible symptom was cp: cannot stat ...: Permission denied -- a message about the file, caused by the directory. Root was unaffected, which is why it survived every local build. The directory is created and chmodded in its own layer before the files arrive, and the db-image job now reads the archive as 1001 immediately after the build so the failure names its own cause. Verified by re-running the job unchanged against 8867b7c, where it had passed, and watching it fail the same way: the cause is the builder on the runner, not the tree. ci:db-image reads /kube-noob-db/ATTRIBUTION.md as --user 1001:0.
G321 A version gate a model evaluates must not be a number comparison. The seven directory skills told Claude to refuse an engine "lower than 0.9.3" and to say "nothing has been audited". 0.10.0 is newer by PEP 440 and lower as text, so the next release turns that instruction into a refusal of a correct engine, in all seven skills at once, with a message asserting no audit happened -- the product's own rule broken in the plugin that advertises it. The gate is now the forModel key, which observes what the engine can do rather than parsing its version, and each skill says not to compare version numbers. tests/test_directory_plugin.py::test_an_older_engine_is_named_as_older_and_not_read now refuses the phrase "lower than" in the block and requires the instruction not to compare.
G322 Fixing one instance of a defect is not fixing the defect. G319 repaired Containerfile.db's COPY --chmod=0444 into a directory the builder creates, and did not look for the same shape elsewhere. Containerfile.seed had it, so /seed was mode 0444, the init container at uid 1001 could not traverse it, and e2e-kind (OpenShift 4.22, Kubernetes 1.35) stayed red on main and every branch for three days with cp: cannot stat '/seed/jira-snapshot.tgz.enc': Permission denied -- the same sentence as G319's, about a different file. The documented seed-image snippet in docs/use-cases.md handed readers the identical defect. The guard is now structural rather than per-file: a COPY --chmod whose mode has no execute bit must be preceded by a RUN mkdir that makes its destination traversable. tests/test_image_copy_modes.py::test_every_chmod_copy_has_a_traversable_destination_directory and ::test_the_documented_snippet_does_not_hand_a_user_the_same_defect, each seen red against each Containerfile with its fix removed.
G328 A caveat a consumer does not read is a caveat that is not there; the honest half of the answer has to travel in the array every reader parses. audit --format sarif on one privileged Deployment emitted twelve results, all of them findings, and put all thirty-six statements about what it had NOT evaluated -- including not evaluated, no Service in the input: KN-N004, ... over fifty-five rules -- into invocations[0].toolExecutionNotifications. That is where SARIF puts what a tool says about its own run, and it is not what the consumers this file exists for parse: GitHub code scanning raises alerts from runs[].results, and DefectDojo and the scanner operators import findings from runs[].results. So the file arrived at the other end as twelve findings and nothing else, and a repository reading 12 alerts had no way to know that fifty-five rules reached no verdict -- an absence reading as a complete answer, one layer out from the verdict, in the format this product leads with. The remedy already existed twice in the same file and had not been carried across: sarif_of_gate emits the gate's incomplete state "as a property of the invocation AND one note-level result, so a consumer that reads only results sees it" (G272), and _not_audited_results emits one kind: review result per object no rule read, "so code scanning lists it in the file view and tracks it". The unevaluated RULES were the neighbour nobody fixed -- docs/review-classes.md class 21, and class 6 (the caveat exists only in JSON) with the JSON one hop further away. The unevaluated rules are now ONE result, not one per reason: a Deployment-only input leaves fifty-five rules unevaluated under thirty-three reasons, and thirty-three near-identical alerts are what gets a rule muted in code scanning, which would switch the signal off more completely than the notification did -- the claim is about the run as a whole, so one alert carries every reason and its fingerprint covers the set, so it changes when coverage changes and closes when nothing is left. A file that was not read is the opposite shape and stays one alert per file with its own location, because that one IS about a place and is fixed file by file. The notifications stay as well: a reader of the invocation keeps what it had, and saying it twice is the better failure of the two. Both new rules say in words how to close the gap and that it is not a pass, because a note-level alert with no remedy is one a reviewer dismisses. Two kind conventions now sit in this file -- the gate's undetermined result omits kind, so SARIF reads it as fail, while these and notAudited use kind: review -- and that is left alone rather than quietly changed, because lowering an existing signal's severity deserves its own decision (docs/open-questions.md) tests/test_sarif_coverage_results.py::test_the_rules_that_reached_no_verdict_are_a_result_and_not_only_a_notification, tests/test_sarif_coverage_results.py::test_the_count_in_the_result_is_the_count_in_the_json, tests/test_sarif_coverage_results.py::test_every_unevaluated_rule_id_is_named_in_the_result, tests/test_sarif_coverage_results.py::test_an_input_that_leaves_nothing_unevaluated_raises_no_coverage_alert, tests/test_sarif_coverage_results.py::test_a_file_that_was_not_read_is_one_result_per_file_with_its_own_location, tests/test_sarif_coverage_results.py::test_the_notifications_are_still_there_so_neither_kind_of_consumer_loses_it, tests/test_sarif_coverage_results.py::test_the_document_is_valid_sarif, tests/test_sarif_coverage_results.py::test_the_coverage_rules_say_what_to_do_and_never_that_it_passed
G327 A tool a profile withheld must read as withheld, never as a tool kube-noob does not have. Fifty-six tools is more than an MCP client lists usefully and more than a model chooses well between, so mcp --tools incident|review|compliance|learn offers fifteen to twenty-three of them. The hazard is not the smaller list; it is what a model concludes from an absence. A client offered the incident profile cannot see kubenoob_fedramp_ksi, and a model that is not told why does not answer "this cannot be checked here" -- it answers the compliance question from its own knowledge, and the user reads an unaudited answer in a session that looked audited. That is this product's own defect, an absence reading as a complete answer, moved from a verdict into the tool surface. So every withheld tool is named in the server's instructions with the flag that offers it, and the instructions say not to answer from knowledge and not to say kube-noob cannot do it. The hand-written instructions are the second source of truth here (class 9): they recommend tools by name, a profile can withhold one of them, and a test holds that every kubenoob_* name the prose mentions is either registered or listed as withheld -- never recommended and silently missing. An unknown --tools value exits 2 rather than falling back to all or to none, because a server offering some other set of tools than the one asked for says so nowhere in the session. A profile decides only what is LISTED: select hands back the same ToolSpec objects, so there is no per-profile variant of a tool to drift, and two servers started differently answer the same call identically. The two reasons a tool is missing stay distinct because they have different fixes -- a profile withheld it (start with another --tools) or a capability tier is off (a decision about what kube-noob may do, which no profile overrides) -- and the tier filter in Toolkit.specs runs first, so a tier-gated tool can never be reported as one a profile hid. config_status is in every profile for that reason. Found while writing it: learn named tutor, which is an MCP prompt and not a tool, so the profile would have offered ten tools while its author believed it offered eleven -- which is why the typo test reads the names from the source rather than from one configuration, since no single load_settings produces the whole surface tests/test_tool_profiles.py::test_a_withheld_tool_is_named_in_the_instructions, tests/test_tool_profiles.py::test_the_prose_never_recommends_a_tool_the_server_does_not_list, tests/test_tool_profiles.py::test_an_unknown_profile_is_a_usage_error_not_a_quiet_fallback, tests/test_tool_profiles.py::test_the_cli_exits_2_on_an_unknown_profile, tests/test_tool_profiles.py::test_no_profile_names_a_tool_that_does_not_exist, tests/test_tool_profiles.py::test_every_tool_is_reachable_through_some_profile, tests/test_tool_profiles.py::test_a_profile_changes_what_is_listed_and_never_what_a_tool_answers, tests/test_tool_profiles.py::test_the_core_tools_are_in_every_profile
G326 A test that measures the runner reports a false failure, which is the mirror image of the false pass this product exists to prevent. G263 replaced two wall-clock budgets with assertions about the code and left thirty-four of the thirty-six in the tree, thirty-one of them below thirty seconds; two of those have since blocked merges. test_hostile_and_oversized_input_is_bounded_and_fast asserted < 1.0 beside an exact cap assertion and measured 1.0239 s and 1.0607 s on PR #77's test (3.11) job, after the identical sha had gone 19 of 19 green on a workflow_dispatch run; the work it timed is 0.001 s, so the budget had a thousandfold headroom and still lost -- it measured a second of descheduling, not a second of work, and the cap beside it was the whole contract, so the clock is gone. test_a_post_that_cannot_get_a_slot_is_refused_with_503_within_the_wait asserted waited < 3 with the server's own SLOT_WAIT_SECONDS patched to 0.3 s: ten times the deadline it claimed to be about, unable to tell an honoured deadline from an ignored one, and it failed at 3.1 s. It now derives its ceiling from that constant, so an ignored deadline (5.0 s shipped) fails and a stall does not. Two more were found by the guard rather than by grep, and are the tightest in the suite: test_no_crafted_line_costs_more_than_a_few_milliseconds asserted a PER-CALL average of 15 ms against a measured 1.2-4.2 ms, under four times, and dividing by the loop count divides out the loop but not a stall. tests/bounds.py is the one helper: a call site states the SHAPE of the regression it guards -- linear, cubic risk, backtracking, a small multiple, fixed work, no hang -- and the shape names the smallest regression as a multiple of the measured honest time; the ceiling is the geometric mean of the two, so the assertion sits as far above the noise as below the regression. The environment enters twice, because the two things that go wrong on a shared runner differ in kind: multiplicatively for a coverage tracer (measured 1.0x on the regex-dominated ceph scan and 1.51x on the Python-heavy SARIF merge here) and additively for a stall, which no multiplier models. Both are frozen at import, so no fixture or test order can move a later ceiling. Every failure prints the elapsed time, the ceiling, its derivation and the regression it is for, because assert 1.06 < 1.0 is why these were dismissed as flakes rather than read. A ceiling that could not catch the regression its shape names raises VacuousBound instead of passing, which is what refused a clock for the ceph site. Three contrast tests, where the signal is the gap between a fast call answered and a fast call queued behind a slow one, could not be made loose: two now assert the ORDER of completion, which no stall can move, and the third states the gap it needs and refuses the call site when the slow side is too small. One site could not be made both meaningful and stable by any ceiling and became a ratio instead, which is G263's own fix: test_diagnose_lists_its_signals_and_stays_fast_on_hostile_text honestly costs 5.0 s, so the linear shape asks for 104 s and MAX_CEILING_SECONDS caps it at the 30 s it already had -- under six times the honest cost, and it measured 32.5 s, 35.7 s and 37.1 s in three of twenty-five runs under load. It now diagnoses half and full of each paste back to back and holds the ratio under 3.0, measured at 1.40 to 1.73 where quadratic is about 4.0. Of the thirty-six, thirty-one went to the helper, one clock was deleted outright, three became ratio or ordering assertions, and one documented sixty-second ceiling was left alone. What none of this catches, and is not asked to: a wrong answer produced quickly, and work that got faster by being skipped -- so every call site keeps its deterministic assertions, and several kept only them. tests/test_timing_assertions.py parses the test tree and refuses a new raw wall-clock comparison below the helper's own loosest ceiling, with no allowlist of the existing sites (class 9); process_time, a ratio between two measurements and a stated catastrophe ceiling are exempt structurally. This is an instance of docs/review-classes.md class 10, recurring as class 21 tests/test_timing_assertions.py::test_no_test_asserts_a_tight_wall_clock_budget, tests/test_timing_assertions.py::test_the_guard_sees_every_shape_of_tight_assertion, tests/test_timing_assertions.py::test_the_guard_lets_through_what_is_not_the_defect, tests/test_ceph_health.py::test_hostile_and_oversized_input_is_bounded_and_fast, tests/test_alert_report_review2.py::test_a_post_that_cannot_get_a_slot_is_refused_with_503_within_the_wait, tests/test_capability_routes.py::test_no_crafted_line_costs_more_than_a_few_milliseconds, tests/test_alert_report_review3.py::test_mcp_reports_waiting_for_a_slot_do_not_starve_other_tools, tests/test_admission.py::test_a_padded_object_does_not_outrun_the_webhooks_timeout
G325 A typo in a key is an unrecognised shape, not an absence, and must not share the absence's answer. apiversion: v1 beside a correct kind: Pod was classified by gate._classify exactly as a GitHub Actions workflow in the same directory is -- "not a Kubernetes manifest (no document with apiVersion and kind)" -- so a privileged pod was listed under skipped, passed stayed true, incomplete stayed false, gate -f DIR exited 0, and verify -f DIR answered allow before the apply. Kubernetes keys are case-sensitive, so the API server would reject that document too; the honest answer is that the file was not read as a manifest, which makes the gate incomplete. A required key that is absent exactly and present as a spelling differing only by letter case, surrounding whitespace or -/_ separators is now named as the typo it is, and that one reason -- marked forbidsVerdict on the entry skipped already carried, not in a second list -- makes passed false, incomplete true, and the exit code 5, beside unreadable's and for G126's reason: a file the walk declined to read is not a policy violation (1). The document is NOT normalised and audited: auditing input the API server would reject as if it were valid is the same false answer pointing the other way. Deliberately narrow -- a kustomization, a Helm values file or a CRD fragment with a kind and no apiVersion at all is not a near miss -- because widening it would turn every repository scanned with gate -f . red. tests/test_near_miss_manifest.py::test_the_gate_exits_five_and_not_one_and_not_zero, ::test_verify_on_a_directory_holding_a_near_miss_is_not_determined, ::test_a_repository_of_non_manifests_beside_one_good_manifest_still_exits_zero (the negative half) and ::test_every_skip_reason_decides_whether_it_forbids_a_verdict, which fails when a reason reaches _classify with no decision beside it in gate.SKIP_REASONS, and ::test_a_file_nested_past_the_loader_limit_forbids_a_verdict_too for the one other reason that decision changed.
G323 A compliance report is only as complete as the rules nobody removed. The OpenShift Compliance Operator's own output is where "compliant" is easiest to get wrong, because four different absences all look like a pass: a rule a TailoredProfile disabled, a ComplianceCheckResult of NOT-APPLICABLE, INFO, MANUAL, ERROR or INCONSISTENT, a rule the profile lists that no result names at all, and a PASS from a ComplianceScan that never reached DONE or reported an error. compliance results therefore answers with the rules the profile SAID would be evaluated, each pass, fail or not-evaluated with the object that proves it, and a ComplianceSuite reporting COMPLIANT over any of them is undetermined -- never compliant -- with the suite's claim printed beside the reason it does not hold. One predicate (undeterminedBecause) decides the headline for the text view, the JSON, the YAML, the MCP result and --require-complete alike, and the pass rate is null with the reason beside it while that list is non-empty, the shape audit.model.Report.undetermined already uses for the score. Two corollaries that are not symmetrical: an absence never makes a pass, and it never withdraws a FAIL the operator did report; and an input with no Compliance Operator objects is undetermined, never "the operator is not installed", which is why this reads files and must-gathers and asks no cluster for anything G331 The file a pipeline uploads is the one that decides what a reviewer sees. G329 A reader of the gate's report must read its verdict, not re-derive one. G272 and G281 made audit and gate answer undetermined for an object the rules did not read and for a rule that declined on one. G335 satisfied is the strongest word an assessment result says, so it has to mean the objective was evidenced. On a report the gate itself called undetermined -- a privileged container in a Deployment under an apiVersion the audit refuses -- three objectives read satisfied/pass: CM-3(2), CM-6 and SA-22. CM-6 said satisfied on 6 clean rules with 27 that reached no verdict. The gap WAS stated, in the finding's description, which is prose a GRC tool does not parse while it reads status.state: review class 6, and the same shape G199 records two branches above (--min-severity critical turning a privileged container into a clean compliance record). Such a control is now EXCLUDED. not-satisfied was the other candidate and is wrong for the same reason satisfied is -- it asserts the objective is not met, and partial evidence does not say that either; an exclusion asserts nothing, and this document already had that mechanism for a control no rule ran over at all, so this extends it from no coverage to partial coverage. The discrimination that makes it usable: a rule that did not run because its SUBJECT is absent ("no Secret in the input") is this report's scope and not a gap in its evidence -- counting those as gaps took a clean input from 23 satisfied objectives to 1, and a compliance report that can never say satisfied is one nobody reads. A rule whose reason is not recorded counts as a gap, because an unknown must not read as a pass. A control with a finding, a withheld rule or an accepted exception is never excluded: a gap elsewhere does not withdraw what was found. The per-control counts are also emitted as props, so the coverage is machine-readable rather than only in the prose. G338 Count the renderers of a report before fixing one of them, and hold them to a predicate rather than to a precedent. The predicates are written as the consumer reads: for SARIF an empty or findings-only runs[].results, for GitLab [], for OSCAL a satisfied/pass objective, for the webhook allowed: true with no warning, for the Markdown and text views a clean masthead with no limits section. Both predictions came true and this row is now history on those points. G340-G343 fixed all four recorded defects, so every strict xfail started passing and failed -- which is what a strict xfail is for -- and the table was emptied in the same pull request rather than left behind. G333 landed too, so the Task is withheld, withheld=True, and the two tests that recorded the open gaps were deleted by their own instructions: test_the_gate_predicate_does_not_count_a_file_it_could_not_read (its docstring said the unreadable disjunct in _withheld "goes away when that test fails") and test_a_kind_no_rule_reads_still_reaches_the_renderers_as_a_pass ("this test then fails, which is the signal to delete it and set withheld=True"). A recorded defect that cannot expire is a second source of truth with no owner; each of these named the condition for its own removal, and that is why removing them was mechanical rather than a judgement call. G333 A container is a container whatever the field is called. unread_pod_specs is the safety net under the kind registries: rather than enumerating the CRD universe it finds the SHAPE of a pod spec and reports the object as not audited, withholding the verdict instead of inventing a finding. That design is right. Its detector required a containers LIST, so three shapes that run containers read as clean: Tekton's spec.steps[] and spec.sidecars[], Argo's spec.templates[].container (one mapping, not a list), and any pod spec carrying only initContainers, which has no containers key at all. A privileged docker:24-dind step in a Tekton Task scored 100.0, exited 0, gated passed and verified allow. The discriminator is unchanged and is the whole guard -- a mapping is container-shaped only if it declares a string image -- so widening which KEY may hold one does not widen what gets accused, and a hit withholds the verdict rather than making a claim. The asymmetry is the argument: recognising one shape too many costs a stated unknown, recognising one too few costs a privileged container reported clean. The release cadence was the one link in the chain with no check on it at all, held only by somebody remembering. release-ready runs weekly and gives three answers, never two: ready when ## Unreleased has bullets and every check on main is green, with the bump_version and tag commands in the issue; not ready naming the checks that are not green; and not known, which is not ready, when a reading could not be taken. Four refusals come before any verdict, each with its own non-zero exit -- an unreadable CHANGELOG.md ("this is not 'nothing to release'"), no release to compare against, checks that could not be read ("whether a release would be safe is NOT KNOWN"), and a main with no check runs at all ("an unanswered question") -- because a report that said ready because it could not look would be this project's own defect pointed at its release. It reads EVERY check rather than the eight required ones, and says why in the issue: gate-ci is the only job that runs the gate as the real Action, as a Tekton Task in kind and as the GitLab template, and it is not required, so a merge queue is green while it is red. A skipped job is not a failure. An empty ## Unreleased closes the issue instead of nagging, which is what bump_version leaves behind after a release took everything. The workflow reports and cannot write to the repository: contents: read, issues: write, checks: read, and a test holds that it runs no git push, git commit or git tag. The counting expression lives in the test as well as the workflow, so a CHANGELOG reshape fails here rather than in a Monday run. The rule for the next writer: a step held together by somebody remembering is not held together -- and when the thing you publish is read from the newest release, the cadence is part of the product tests/test_a_release_nobody_cuts.py::test_the_cadence_is_checked_weekly_and_can_be_asked_by_hand, tests/test_a_release_nobody_cuts.py::test_the_awk_counts_only_what_is_waiting_to_be_released, tests/test_a_release_nobody_cuts.py::test_every_check_counts_not_only_the_required_eight, tests/test_a_release_nobody_cuts.py::test_a_reading_that_could_not_be_taken_is_not_ready, tests/test_a_release_nobody_cuts.py::test_nothing_to_release_closes_the_issue_instead_of_nagging, tests/test_a_release_nobody_cuts.py::test_the_ready_answer_carries_the_command_and_the_not_ready_answer_carries_the_reason, tests/test_a_release_nobody_cuts.py::test_it_cannot_write_to_the_repository_itself, tests/test_a_release_nobody_cuts.py::test_the_real_changelog_is_readable_by_the_same_expression

G359 A receipt reads the verdict the report carries; it must not read a key the report stopped writing, and it must not recompute an exit code the command already decided (review classes 4, 9 and 21). G310's rule is that a receipt records the verdict it was given and never re-judges it. The rule held and the report moved: receipt.audit_exit_code, which audit returns, read summary.scoreWithheld, the name G272 gave the undetermined reason and G281 retired for summary.verdict: undetermined + summary.verdictReason. The key was then absent, absent read as "not withheld", and an undetermined audit with nothing at --fail-on would have exited 0 with a receipt recording 0 and a null score beside no reason. receipt.audit_undetermined reads the two keys the report writes, and the word decides: undetermined with no reason is still exit 1. For the gate, receipt.gate_exit_code is the ONE place the code is decided -- returned by gate, recorded by the receipt, recomputed by receipt verify -- read off model.open_unread (G340), model.blocked_manifests (G325) and passed; before, the receipt was written ahead of the G325 branch and recorded 1 for a run that exited 5, and the replay carried its own arithmetic and dropped --skip-unreadable, so a waived run verified as DIFFERS. The reason a gate receipt records is model.incomplete_reason, so all three ways a gate is incomplete are named and the word beside it is gate.verdict_word's. The receipt field is verdict.undetermined (was scoreWithheld, never released). A gate receipt's assumptions and exceptedFindings are read off the report rather than written as empty. The receipt documents are renderers of the verdict and have adapters in the conformance suite; verify_receipt states no verdict over its input and is exempted with what it owes. Not changed: sarif_of_gate and gate.run still spell the forbidsVerdict filter inline where blocked_manifests now exists tests/test_receipts.py::test_an_undetermined_audit_is_not_exit_0_because_a_key_was_renamed_under_the_receipt, tests/test_receipts.py::test_a_determined_audit_keeps_its_exit_code_and_its_score, tests/test_receipts.py::test_the_one_exit_code_rule_lives_in_one_place, tests/test_receipts.py::test_the_receipt_records_the_score_the_command_gave_and_never_a_second_judgement, tests/test_receipts.py::test_a_gate_receipt_records_exit_5_for_a_file_the_walk_declined_to_read, tests/test_receipts.py::test_a_gate_receipt_is_reproduced_whatever_made_the_gate_incomplete, tests/test_receipts.py::test_a_waived_unread_file_does_not_withhold_the_gate_receipts_verdict, tests/test_receipts.py::test_a_gate_that_exits_5_still_has_a_verdict_and_the_receipt_records_that_code, tests/test_receipts.py::test_the_gates_exit_code_is_read_off_the_report_in_one_place, tests/test_receipts.py::test_a_gate_receipt_records_the_assumptions_and_the_excepted_count_the_gate_reported, tests/test_receipts.py::test_the_mcp_verifier_reproduces_an_undetermined_receipt, tests/test_renderer_conformance.py::test_renderer_conformance, tests/test_renderer_conformance.py::test_every_renderer_has_an_adapter, tests/test_control_plane_review7.py::test_an_undetermined_audit_exits_1_whatever_fail_on_says

G379 An exception that accepts an object nothing read bought a cleaner answer than not having the object at all (review classes 8, 9 and 21). AGENTS.md says a waiver is never a cleaner answer about the same evidence. Measured on 78b0484 with the real CLI at --fail-on medium, it was: a hardened three-replica Deployment with probes and no NetworkPolicy exited 1 with score 94.0 and KN-N001 open; the same Deployment beside an allow-all projectcalico.org/v3 NetworkPolicy exited 1 with the score withheld, which is right; and the same two files plus a notAudited exception accepting that policy exited 0 with score 98.0 and NO findings. Adding an object the rules cannot read, then waiving it, scored four points higher than the cleaner input and passed a gate the cleaner input fails. Before G368 the three inputs gave 94 / withheld / 94-with-KN-N001-open, so this is a regression G368 and G369 introduced rather than a standing defect. The seam is in _all_accepted (audit/model.py), written for G281 review 5: Report.undetermined stops a decline withholding the score once an exception accepts every object the rule declined on. That was sound while a decline only ever meant "the rule found nothing there because it did not look". G368 and G369 made a decline able to stand IN FOR a finding -- KN-N001 now declines instead of reporting "No NetworkPolicy for namespace prod", because the namespace may be isolated by a policy nothing could read -- and the acceptance then re-opened the score while the suppressed finding stayed suppressed, scoring the rule as though it had been evaluated and found nothing. Accepting an object says the reader may stop asking to read it; it does not say what the rule would have found in it. So AuditContext.finding_suppressed records the rules whose decline REPLACED a finding, at the eight in-rule call sites that decline instead of yielding, engine.py writes suppressedFinding: true on those declined rows only (absent reads as "no", which is what every decline meant before this key existed), and _all_accepted returns False for such a row however the object was accepted: the report stays undetermined, which is the true answer and neither the one the old code gave nor the one before G368. The distinction is per decline, not per rule and not per report, because the accept path is the only way a Calico, Istio or Knative shop ever reaches a verdict and blunting it would push every such user onto a flag that turns the check off -- the positive control is a refused dra.k8s.io/v1 ResourceClaim no rule reasons from the absence of, whose waiver still restores the score to 98.0 and exit 0, unchanged. The two methods moved from rules.py to audit/evidence.py as module-level functions with thin delegates left behind, because the new field put rules.py over the plugin directory's 256 KiB per-file cap. Not covered: a decline the engine records for a rule that never reached its own call site is still coverable by an acceptance, which is correct but means the marking lives at the call sites rather than in the IR, and the coverage ledger docs/report-ir.md proposes -- where the obligation, not the object, is the key -- would carry this distinction as data instead. tests/test_a_waiver_is_never_a_cleaner_answer.py::test_the_cleaner_input_is_the_baseline, tests/test_a_waiver_is_never_a_cleaner_answer.py::test_an_unreadable_policy_alone_withholds_the_verdict, tests/test_a_waiver_is_never_a_cleaner_answer.py::test_a_waiver_does_not_buy_a_better_answer_than_the_cleaner_input, tests/test_a_waiver_is_never_a_cleaner_answer.py::test_the_waived_report_is_undetermined_rather_than_clean, tests/test_a_waiver_is_never_a_cleaner_answer.py::test_the_suppressing_decline_is_marked_as_such, tests/test_a_waiver_is_never_a_cleaner_answer.py::test_a_waiver_over_a_decline_that_suppressed_nothing_still_restores_the_score, tests/test_a_waiver_is_never_a_cleaner_answer.py::test_that_decline_is_not_marked_as_suppressing

Two defects in the mechanism walk, both found by red-teaming it. The vacuous pass. That last pair also answers the general question: a hop IS reported holds when its rules all abstained silently, because a rule that skips its subject records nothing in notEvaluated and nothing in findings, so the walk cannot tell it from a rule that read the object and was satisfied -- which is why these hops may claim only what the rules test for. G397 KN-C007 was excused by an autoscaler that can hold the workload at one replica, and said nothing about it (review classes 1 and 13). The rule fires on a Deployment, StatefulSet or ReplicaSet declaring fewer than two replicas, and its own rationale is "one replica means downtime on every node drain, upgrade or node failure". _has_hpa excused it whenever ANY HorizontalPodAutoscaler's scaleTargetRef named the workload, and never read that autoscaler's minReplicas -- which the API defaults to 1. An HPA at 1..5 lets the workload idle at one replica; an HPA at 1..1 pins it there. Measured on main before this change: one replica with either autoscaler gave no KN-C007 finding and no notEvaluated entry. Why nothing caught it. G395 could only make the hop claim less -- its title became "...without an autoscaler" and its sentence disclosed that "KN-C007 does not read that autoscaler's own minimum" -- because the gap was in the rule, not the walk, and G395 pinned the rule's silence with a stale-detector so the fix would announce itself. The repair reads the floor the way KN-C014 in the same module already reads it -- minReplicas if it is an int, the API default of 1 otherwise -- and excuses the finding only at a floor of two or more. G395's stale-detector fired exactly as written ("KN-C007 now speaks here; this test is stale") and was updated to assert the new truth: on one replica with an HPA pinned at one, the hop FAILS and the walk is broken, exit 1, which is what a workload that cannot survive its node should be told. Then analyze PASSED on main at 340d243, on v4.39.4. One pass against one fail proves nothing; the run history does: on 2026-11-25 every one of 25 CodeQL runs on v4.39.3 was green, and 2 of the 5 runs on v4.39.4 (counting #127's own pull request) failed, both at the same step. So v4.39.4 is intermittently broken, not deterministically, and the PR's green and 340d243's green are the same flake landing the other way. The log shows where: the analysis completes, then v4.39.4 runs "Combining SARIF files using the CodeQL CLI" over the job's two language runs (python, actions), warns "Column kind mismatch between SARIF files", and the upload step fails with no message. GitHub has documented that code scanning stopped combining multiple SARIF runs uploaded in one file. A single-run upload is unaffected -- scorecard.yml's upload-sarif stays on v4.39.4 and its check is green -- so only codeql.yml's init and analyze move back, together, with the reason written beside them. .github/dependabot.yml ignores that one release only, so the next one is still proposed. Because the failure is intermittent, one green run on that pull request will not show it is fixed; take it after several. The rule for the next merge: "everything green" is every check, not the required eight. A non-required job is still a check, and a security scanner that cannot upload is a scanner whose results nobody sees. ci:analyze, .github/workflows/codeql.yml, .github/dependabot.yml
G396 A Pod that named five objects none of which were in the input audited at exit 0, and nothing said so -- and the first repair fired HIGH on every correct GitOps repository in the suite. A Pod naming a ConfigMap volume, a Secret volume, a PersistentVolumeClaim, a ServiceAccount and an envFrom.configMapRef, none present, reported one unrelated LOW finding (KN-S014) and 61 rules not evaluated, in a single file AND in a directory: the cause was not an abstention but an absent rule. KN-C013 is about a pull secret in the WRONG namespace and KN-C015 reads only ResourceClaims, so no rule asked the question, and the accounting invariant could not see that -- every rule was in a bucket. The first KN-C017 fired on every referenced name a whole-namespace directory did not define. The full suite answered with 98 failures, every one a correct repository: 82 in test_verify.py (each good/ directory went from allow to deny), the gate's own good/ fixture (Secret 'orders-db'), the bundle generate writes (Secret 'app-tls'), a hardened Pod's ServiceAccount 'ok', the renderers and the hook. All three distinct names were of a kind the namespace defined NONE of. That is why the first design was wrong: cli._partial_input takes a directory to be the whole namespace, which is sound for what people commit (a NetworkPolicy, a PodDisruptionBudget) and unsound for what they do not -- a correct GitOps repository commits no Secrets (sealed-secrets, external-secrets, cert-manager or a person create them) and often no ServiceAccounts or claims, so a gate that fired there would be turned off, a worse defect than the false pass. The rule now judges a (namespace, kind) only where the bundle defines at least one object of that kind there: a name with nothing behind it is then a real gap, most often a typo, and the message names the objects that ARE defined (db-creds beside a reference to db-cred) and says "unless something outside this bundle creates it", so it stays true where a producer the rules do not read made the Secret. A kind the namespace defines none of is not judged and the record says which (notEvaluated: "the bundle defines no ConfigMap or PersistentVolumeClaim or Secret or ServiceAccount in namespace shop, so a name a pod spec gives there may be an object created outside the bundle ... and its absence is not evidence"); a fragment abstains as before ("part of a namespace was passed, so an object that lives in a file you did not pass is not an absent one"). So the reproduction itself exits 0 again -- named under notEvaluated, not passed -- and the same Pod in a namespace that manages the four kinds fires five HIGH findings at exit 1. Secret stays in scope on evidence: the suite's two Secret false positives were both in namespaces defining no Secret, which the gate removes, and the defined-some-referenced-a-missing-one typo is the case a reader most needs caught; the residue -- a namespace that commits some Secrets while a producer makes others -- is answered by the message's "unless", because reading cert-manager, SealedSecret or ExternalSecret objects would mean declaring kinds outside SUBJECT_GROUPS, which test_absent_kinds.py refuses and which would change what every rule refuses. A rule that fires on one kind and does not judge another RAN, so it is partly evaluated, and run_rules gave such a rule's note from ctx.declined, which nothing_to_judge never writes: the note fell back to "a field in this input has a type it cannot read", false about this input; it reads no_verdict_reasons before that fallback now. Out of scope by design: cluster-scoped referents (a directory is a namespace, never the cluster) and imagePullSecrets (an absent one does not stop the Pod; KN-C013's field). How a kind the rules do not audit is read. The published tables said this on purpose -- "a stopped walk reached no verdict about the path, so it is never 1" -- and that sentence was false whenever a break came first: AGENTS.md (G272) makes 1 "a verdict was reached and it is not a pass", and a broken hop is such a verdict about the path, because no hop after it can make the path work. audit already behaves this way: findings beside rules that reached no verdict exit 1, not 5. The repair makes a break win over a later stop: the verdict is broken at 1, stoppedAt still names the hop that could not be judged, the hops after it are still counted as not walked, and the reason names both ("these hops are broken: replicas-can-land-apart; the walk then stopped at the-budget-lets-one-go ..."). The rule the walk now follows, stated in the module docstring: it collects the namespace of every object of a kind an observable hop names in needs or reads, publishes them as namespaces, and prints them on the second line of the text view. Exactly one is the namespace judged. More than one stops the walk at the first observable hop -- cannot judge, the namespaces named and quoted, exit 5 -- because nothing in the input says which one the reader meant, and a hop's rules answer for the whole report rather than for one namespace of it. Runs are cancelled for pull requests only now. The test pins the expression. .github/workflows/ci.yml, tests/test_video_workflows.py
G412 G398's Dependabot ignore matched nothing, so the version it was written to keep out was proposed the next day (review class 13). G398 pinned codeql.yml back to v4.39.3 and added ignore: {dependency-name: github/codeql-action, versions: [4.39.4]}. The workflow uses the sub-actions github/codeql-action/init and github/codeql-action/analyze, and Dependabot matches dependency-name against those exact names, so the entry matched neither and #140 proposed 4.39.4 for both. An ignore that matches nothing is a no-op that reads as protection. The repair is the wildcard github/codeql-action*, which matches every sub-action of that repository, and its comment now says "intermittently (2 of 5 runs)" as G398's corrected measurement does. The test reads every uses: in the workflows and requires each github-actions ignore entry to match at least one (fnmatch, the same glob Dependabot documents), plus that both pinned CodeQL sub-actions are covered; against the old file 2 of 3 fail. A guard asserts the uses: reader found actions/checkout and the CodeQL sub-action, so a broken reader cannot make every ignore look dead or alive. #140 is closed with a pointer here. .github/dependabot.yml, tests/test_an_ignore_that_matches_nothing.py
G413 release-ready could not file its own report: the label it files under did not exist (review class 21). Its first run, dispatched on 2026-11-26 after G399 and G403 merged, took every reading -- the changelog, the latest release, the checks on main, the release run -- wrote its answer, and exited 1 on could not add label: 'release-ready' not found, with no issue opened. The workflow had only ever been read by its tests, whose stub gh accepted any label; GitHub's issue create --label refuses one the repository lacks, and no step had created it. risk-review creates its own labels with gh label create --force; this one never did. The repair creates the label (--force, so an existing one is left alone) immediately before issue create, on that branch only, after every reading is in -- so a reading that is not known still writes nothing, labels included. Tests: the stub now records label create, every create is asserted to be preceded by it, an existing issue is edited with no label write, and with the label step removed 5 of the file's tests fail. .github/workflows/release-ready.yml, tests/test_a_release_that_did_not_finish_shipping.py
G419 The admission webhook answered with more warning lines than it documents (review class 13; found by admission-kind on #145). Config.max_warnings caps the findings, and the response carries at most max_warnings + 1 lines -- tests/test_admission.py and the in-cluster load run (scripts/admission_kind_load.py) both assert it. The two statements, "rules that need other objects of the namespace were not evaluated" and "not fully audited, so this is not a clean result", were appended on top of max_warnings findings and the "N more findings" line, so an object with ten findings and a rule that declined got twelve. Latent since the statements were added; reachable in the load run once G407 made a non-numeric replicas a decline, and its fuzz cases 27 and 81 failed. The repair never drops a statement -- each says something the findings cannot -- so the statements take their room from the findings, "N more findings" counts every finding not shown, and when max_warnings is smaller than the number of statements they share one line. Tests run the load run's own fuzz generator in-process with its own seed: no case over the bound at max_warnings 1, 2 and 10, and the not-fully-audited statement still present in 79 of 600 cases; fuzz case 27 itself keeps its statement, its findings and one "more findings" line. With the old assembly 4 fail. src/kube_noob/admission.py, tests/test_the_webhook_keeps_its_warning_bound.py
G420 A fourth mechanism lesson, "A Pod calls a Service", written from measurements (kubelearn's in-cluster call). A pod asks cluster DNS for a Service name, gets a ClusterIP, and the node sends it to a ready endpoint: seven hops, two of which no manifest records (the DNS answer and the ClusterIP translation) and are observable: false. Every rule was run before the lesson was written, with its object present and fine, present and violating, and absent. KN-N013 is silent and evaluated on an ingress-only policy and on an Egress policy allowing UDP and TCP 53, fires on an Egress policy with no route to 53, and says "no networking.k8s.io NetworkPolicy in the input" when there is none -- so the policy hop (KN-N001, which fires on that absence) goes first and the DNS hop then stops instead of holding on nothing. KN-V023 fires on a numeric targetPort the pods do not declare; KN-V011, KN-V012 and KN-V023 say "no pod spec in the input" without a workload, from a directory and from a file alike, and with no pod spec the first two hops' rules abstain too, so the walk stops at its first hop with nothing judged. Only KN-V011 is an absent: rule on the Service hop; KN-V023 compares a Service with pods that are here, so its finding stands on a fragment. Fixtures tests/fixtures/mechanism/service/complete/bundle.yaml and tests/fixtures/mechanism/service/broken/bundle.yaml walk complete at 0 and broken at 1 (KN-N013 and KN-V023 both named). Tests pin the measurement table, the no-policy and no-workload cases, the ingress-only policy, CLI and MCP parity and the HTML view. src/kube_noob/data/curriculum.yaml, tests/test_a_pod_calls_a_service.py, tests/fixtures/mechanism/service/complete/bundle.yaml
G430 The page of a mechanism walk was a grid of boxes with three buttons, and looked nothing like the lesson player it was meant to follow (review classes 6, 9 and 13). G402 drew learn mechanism --format html as a schematic: one bordered box per hop, "Previous hop / Play / Next hop", and a banner. It is now a port of KubeLearn's lesson player (MIT, Copyright (c) 2026 Joe Sabbagh), driven by the same dict walk() returns: a stage with an isometric scene, a caption strip and a transport bar; a 360px story column ("Hop n of N", the hop's title and whole explanation, a facts table of object, rules, verdict and findings, then the list of hops); a Cluster / Sequence toggle; an Events drawer of the evidence; dark and light themes; and an overview as step 0 that replaces the banner (path verdict, exit code and its meaning, the four counts, the walk's reason, the namespace judged). The numbering skips G421-G429 on purpose: another branch is adding rows there. The scene stands on the reader's own objects. A hop that names an object stands on a card with the kind's badge (ING, SVC, DEP, NP, SA, ROLE, RB, HPA, STS, PVC and the rest), the object's name and its namespace; two hops that read one object share its card and the card shows the worse of them; a kind this build has no badge for gets its capitals, and a kind out of the input is never a class name (tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_the_scene_has_one_card_per_object_a_hop_names, tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_a_card_two_hops_stand_on_shows_the_worse_of_them, tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_a_kind_this_build_has_no_badge_for_gets_its_capitals_and_never_markup, tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_two_different_objects_at_one_spot_of_a_layout_do_not_stand_on_each_other). A thing no manifest holds is drawn as one. The visitor's laptop, cluster DNS, the node's packet path, the scheduler, the API server and the worker nodes drawn as scenery are KubeLearn's external and node actors, each with a dashed outline and the words "not in any manifest"; scenery has no hop, so it has no verdict (tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_what_no_manifest_holds_is_dashed_and_says_so, tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_the_request_lesson_draws_its_unobservable_last_hop_as_a_node). A verdict is a glyph and a word wherever it is shown -- a tick only beside a hop that holds, a cross and BROKEN, ? and cannot judge, a hollow ring and "not checked by any manifest", and "not walked" -- on the card, in the list, in the facts, in the sequence view and in the drawer. KubeLearn colours a Service card green; here no kind of object is green, because a card with no verdict yet must not look like a pass (tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_a_broken_hop_carries_the_cross_and_the_word_on_its_card_and_in_the_list, tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_every_state_in_the_list_is_a_glyph_and_a_word_and_only_a_hold_gets_the_tick, tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_green_is_kept_for_a_hop_that_holds). A stopped walk greys what it did not walk. The hops after a stop are drawn as not walked from the start, on cards of their own and never on the card of a walked hop that reads the same kind; the marker is drawn only for a hop a rule read and auto-play never advances into an unwalked hop (tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_a_stopped_walk_greys_the_hops_after_the_stop_and_never_marks_them_holding). Without the script the page is still the whole walk: the overview, every hop with its verdict and its explanation uncut (G411 held 200 characters in the text view; this view capped at 600), the list and the evidence table are in the HTML as written, and the scene is drawn in its final state (tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_without_javascript_the_page_lists_every_hop_its_verdict_and_its_whole_explanation, tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_an_explanation_longer_than_the_old_cap_is_on_the_page_whole, tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_the_overview_is_step_zero_and_says_what_the_exit_code_means, tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_the_drawer_lists_the_evidence_the_walk_recorded). Every lesson is drawn. The four shipped lessons have a layout by role (edge actors front right, traffic cards in front of the nodes, workloads and policy behind), a test fails when a walkable lesson has none, and a lesson with no layout -- or a walk whose hops are not its lesson's -- is drawn as a plain isometric row rather than refused (tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_every_lesson_renders_on_its_fixtures_the_same_twice_and_small, tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_the_walks_reach_every_exit_code_and_every_state, tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_a_lesson_with_no_layout_is_drawn_as_a_row_and_not_refused). What G402 held still holds, unchanged: no URL on the page (not even the ported project's), the same Content-Security-Policy, the same bytes for the same walk, nothing from a manifest as markup -- now in SVG text too -- and the walk's exit code in every format (tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_a_hostile_object_name_is_text_in_the_scene_and_in_the_sequence, tests/test_a_walk_you_can_watch.py::test_the_page_names_no_address_and_forbids_fetching_one, tests/test_a_walk_you_can_watch.py::test_no_string_from_the_walk_becomes_markup_or_a_command, tests/test_a_walk_you_can_watch.py::test_every_format_exits_with_the_walks_exit_code). Two assertions of that file changed because they pinned the old layout: the closed list of tags the page may contain (the player adds SVG shapes and tspan, a table, a dialog) and the set of links (#overview beside #step-N). The player: next and previous hop, auto-play, speed, restart, zoom, arrow keys, Space, Home and ?, #step-N and #overview, a theme toggle kept in localStorage inside a try, and no flight or camera easing under prefers-reduced-motion (tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_the_player_has_the_controls_the_keys_and_the_deep_links, tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_both_themes_are_in_the_stylesheet_and_no_font_is_fetched, tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_the_isometric_helpers_write_the_same_bytes_and_no_negative_zero). The port is credited: the MIT notice is in THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md, each ported module names its source, and every page says "Player design ported from KubeLearn by Joe Sabbagh, MIT" as text (tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_the_port_is_credited_where_a_licence_asks_for_it). While the walk is on a hop (found by reviewing the first cut against KubeLearn's screenshots): the camera box holds the hop's two actors whole, label included, is at least 520 wide, leaves the bottom of the stage clear for the caption strip and the transport bar, keeps the plate in view, and takes in any actor it would cut through rather than crop it; the marker's chip is moved off any actor label it would cover; the card the walk is standing on shows THAT hop's verdict, with an earlier break on the same card kept on its second line as hop 1 and a cross, while the overview and the page without the script show the worse of the two; a card for a kind the walk names no object for takes the name its findings give it (one object: that object; several: the first and how many more; another kind's object is not borrowed), and the Object fact says the name came from the finding; and a hop that stays on the card of the hop before it is captioned 02/07 at Ingress/shop/shop, not as a journey from an object to itself (tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_the_camera_on_a_hop_crops_no_actor_and_keeps_the_caption_band_clear, tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_the_chip_on_the_marker_is_moved_off_a_label_it_would_cover, tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_the_chip_is_clear_of_every_label_on_the_shipped_walks, tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_the_card_under_the_walk_shows_this_hops_verdict_and_keeps_an_earlier_break_beside_it, tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_a_card_for_a_kind_takes_the_name_the_evidence_gives_it, tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_several_objects_in_the_evidence_are_counted_and_another_kind_is_not_borrowed, tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_a_hop_that_stays_on_its_card_is_at_it_and_not_a_journey_from_it_to_itself). Not held by a test: how the page looks. It was compared by eye with KubeLearn's player at 1440x800 dark and light and at 390x844, and the marker's flight, the camera and the keys were driven in headless Chromium, on the day it was written; no test opens a browser. src/kube_noob/learn/mechanism_html.py, src/kube_noob/learn/mechanism_scene.py, src/kube_noob/learn/mechanism_player.py, src/kube_noob/learn/iso.py, tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py, tests/test_a_walk_you_can_watch.py, THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md
G421 A fifth mechanism lesson, "A packet meets a NetworkPolicy", written from measurements (kubelearn's NetworkPolicy walkthrough). Default allow, the policy is written, the CNI enforces it, the drop, the allowed call: six hops, two of which no manifest records (which CNI enforces the policy, and the per-packet decision) and are observable: false. Every rule was run before the lesson was written, with its object present and fine, present and violating, and absent. KN-N001 fires with no NetworkPolicy (and beside a CiliumNetworkPolicy alone), declines on a fragment and with no workload; KN-N012, KN-N007 and KN-N013 all say "no networking.k8s.io NetworkPolicy in the input" without one -- so the namespace hop goes first, a namespace with no policy is BROKEN there, and the next hop stops instead of holding on nothing. Three shapes were measured silent on every rule, and each is written into the sentence of the hop that would otherwise have claimed it: a second workload that no policy selects (KN-N012 reads policy to pods, never pods to policy), an ingress rule that names ports and no from (KN-N007 fires on {}, from: [], namespaceSelector: {} and 0.0.0.0/0, and not on that), and a policy port no container declares (no rule compares them, so no hop is titled for ports; the per-packet hop says it). No hop that holds says a packet is admitted: a default-deny walks complete. KN-N012 is the only absent: rule, so a selector that matches nothing is cannot judge on a fragment and BROKEN on a namespace. Fixtures tests/fixtures/mechanism/networkpolicy/complete/bundle.yaml and tests/fixtures/mechanism/networkpolicy/broken/bundle.yaml walk complete at 0 and broken at 1 (KN-N012 and KN-N007 each named on its own hop). Tests pin the seventeen-row measurement table, that every rule a hop names has a row in it, the no-policy, no-workload, single-file and empty-input cases, the three stated limits, the default-deny wording, CLI and MCP parity hop by hop, the text view and the HTML view; with KN-N007 taken off its hop 2 fail. src/kube_noob/data/curriculum.yaml, tests/test_a_packet_meets_a_network_policy.py, tests/fixtures/mechanism/networkpolicy/complete/bundle.yaml
G422 A sixth mechanism lesson, "Deploy an app", written from measurements (kubelearn's first walkthrough). Send the manifest, gatekeeping, saved to etcd, the controllers react, scheduling, the kubelet starts it, ready for traffic: nine hops here, four of which a running cluster decides (the API server admitting and storing the object, the controllers creating the pods, the scheduler binding one, the kubelet starting it) and are observable: false. Every rule was run first with its object fine, violating and absent. KN-V001, KN-V002, KN-V005, KN-V009, KN-V010 and KN-C006 each fire on their own violation and are silent and evaluated on a healthy Deployment; they share the first hop, whose title names those four things and whose sentence says they are not the whole of the API server's validation. KN-V010 is on the first hop and not on a controllers hop: it is a validation finding about the Deployment, and by G406 a hop that reads a Deployment does not hold beside one, so placed later it would have turned a selector mismatch into a stop at exit 5 instead of a break at 1. KN-C004 fires with no requests, on a fragment too. KN-C017 has three answers and the hop needs all three: silent when the name is here, a finding when the name is missing beside another object of that kind, and no verdict when the bundle defines no object of that kind at all (an operator may create it) or when a fragment was passed -- so the hop stops in both, needs no absent: entry, and a single file never walks complete. KN-S012 fires on :latest, on a version tag and on no image; KN-C001 on no readiness probe. With no workload, or a Deployment with no pod template, the pod-spec rules say "no pod spec in the input" and the first hop stops with nothing held. One gap was measured and not closed here: containers: [] is silent on every rule and on the schema stage although the API server refuses it, so such a Deployment walks complete; the table pins it so a rule that starts reading it fails this file first. Fixtures tests/fixtures/mechanism/deploy/complete/bundle.yaml and tests/fixtures/mechanism/deploy/broken/bundle.yaml walk complete at 0 and broken at 1 (KN-C017, KN-S012 and KN-C001, each on its own hop). Tests pin the twenty-row measurement table, that every rule a hop names has a row where it fires, each remaining rule breaking its own hop, the no-Deployment, no-template, no-kind-here, single-file and break-then-stop cases, the wording of the hops that hold, CLI and MCP parity hop by hop, the text view and the HTML view; with KN-S012 or KN-V010 taken off its hop 2 fail. src/kube_noob/data/curriculum.yaml, tests/test_deploy_an_app_walks.py, tests/fixtures/mechanism/deploy/complete/bundle.yaml
G423 A walkable lesson had no way to say which bundles show it, so every consumer listed them by hand. The website drew each mechanism lesson beside example bundles hard-coded on its side; a lesson added here (G420, G421, G422) had none there until someone remembered, and nothing in this repository knew which fixture belongs to which lesson. The repair is an optional examples: list on a lesson -- fixture directories relative to the repository's test fixtures -- set on all six walkable lessons, and mechanism.examples() to read it. It is read as strictly as a step: a non-empty list of distinct relative paths made of lowercase segments, so .., a leading or trailing /, a backslash, an uppercase letter, a newline, a dot-file and an address all raise, and walk calls the reader so a lesson with a malformed field is refused rather than walked. The walk's answer does not carry the field and the text view does not print it: the wheel ships no tests, and a path the installed package does not have is not an answer. Found while writing it: mech-cp-a-node-goes-dark has no example of its own -- it borrows the request-path bundles, and both are BROKEN at the spread hop and then stop at the budget hop for want of a PodDisruptionBudget. That is recorded in the table, not repaired here. Tests write out every (lesson, example, exit code) and fail by name on a lesson without examples or an example whose verdict moved; each example must be a directory holding a manifest whose walk reaches complete or broken; seventeen malformed values are refused by the reader and by the walk; a lesson without the field still walks; and the walk's JSON and text carry no example path. With the call removed from walk 17 fail, and with the segment pattern loosened 10 fail. src/kube_noob/learn/mechanism.py, src/kube_noob/data/curriculum.yaml, tests/test_a_lesson_names_its_examples.py
